30:## Service boundaries (owned by 03, read by 04)
31-
32-Set by 03 during Phase 1 planning (full reasoning: `03-engineering-delivery/PLAN.md` §4).
33-
34-- **Two binaries**, not one: `cmd/api-service` (Q1 DAO + Q2 REST API, client-facing, bearer-token auth) and `cmd/idp-connector` (Q3, outbound-only to vendors) — different threat models/auth surfaces per 02, independent scaling needs, mirrors the assignment's own Q2/Q3 split.
35-- **Package layout:**
36-  ```
37-  cmd/api-service/main.go
38-  cmd/idp-connector/main.go
39-  internal/dao/        # Repository composite interface (Profiles/Credentials/Methods) + postgres/cockroachdb/sqlite implementations
40-  internal/model/      # shared structs: UserProfile, UserCredential
41-  internal/api/         # REST handlers, router, auth middleware
42-  internal/connector/   # /auth, /identity client + handlers
43-  internal/config/      # env-var config loading, shared by both binaries
44-  internal/app/         # shared bootstrap (route registration, config wiring) both cmd/*/main.go wrap thinly
45-  ```
46-- **Config surface:** environment variables only (12-factor: config lives in the environment, not in checked-in files) — `DB_DRIVER` (postgres|cockroachdb|sqlite), `DB_DSN`, `DB_DSN_FILE` (file-mount path; takes precedence over `DB_DSN` when both are set, per 02's `handoff-04-secrets.md` S7 revision — a DSN's embedded password is as exposed by `kubectl describe`/crash dumps/env-in-child-process as any other secret in that inventory, so `DB_DSN` is now a transitional fallback, not a permanent equal option), `HTTP_ADDR`, `AUTH_JWT_ISSUER` = `https://auth.loginid-takehome.internal` / `AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE` = `loginid-api-service` (literal values per `handoff-03-auth.md` — not secrets, safe to ship in config), `CONNECTOR_JWT_AUDIENCE` = `idp-connector-service` (the connector's own inbound audience, distinct from `AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE` — `handoff-03-auth.md` v3 / `connector-security.md` §5, consistency-pass F15), `CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID`/`CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET` (api-service's own client credential for obtaining a `connector:identity-lookup`-scoped token to call idp-connector on a caller's behalf — a secret, added to `handoff-04-secrets.md`'s inventory as row 10 per F15), `APP_MODE` = `verifier` (default) | `issuer` (selects which of `api-service`'s two Kubernetes Deployments a given pod is — the issuer Deployment alone mounts the JWT signing-key Secret, per 02/04's F13 fix in `handoff-04-secrets.md`/`containerization-design.md`; confirmed spelling from Theo), `IDP_ABC_BASE_URL`, `IDP_ABC_CLIENT_ID`/`IDP_ABC_CLIENT_SECRET`. No flags, no config files.
47-- **DAO driver-selection mechanism:** a factory `dao.New(driver, dsn string) (dao.Repository, error)` chosen at startup from `DB_DRIVER`. `dao.Repository` is a composite interface — `Profiles() ProfileRepository`, `Credentials() CredentialRepository`, `Methods() AuthMethodRepository`, plus `CreateProfileWithCredential(ctx, p, c)`, `DeleteExpired(ctx, class, olderThan, maxRows)`, `DeleteProfile(ctx, id, externalRef)`, and `Close() error` directly on the composite — matching 05's now-accepted contract in `multi-db-strategy.md` §1/§3b/§3c (the retention-sweep addendum, consistency-pass F21), kept deliberately distinct (not one flat interface) because it's a load-bearing PII/credential-separation control per `05-data-ops/pii-governance.md`: a handler has to reach for the credential accessor on purpose. Each backend package returns one struct satisfying the composite. Factory signature and `DB_DRIVER` selection are unchanged.
48-- **Stable for 04 to build against:** the two-binary split, the `cmd/` entrypoint names, the env-var config approach and variable names above, and the `dao.New(driver, dsn)` factory signature. **05's contract has landed and is accepted** (consistency-pass F23 — this line previously said the composite's method set was "not yet stable" pending the contract; it has since landed, including `CreateProfileWithCredential` and `Close()` above). **Not yet stable:** internal DAO implementation details, ORM/query-builder choice, handler-package internals — those are implementation, not contract.
49-- **Note for 04, from the Go-layout three-hats ruling (`03-engineering-delivery/decisions/go-layout-debate.md`):** "two binaries" is a *process* boundary, not a claim about host/deploy placement — whether `api-service` and `idp-connector` land on the same node is 04's call, not implied by this section. If they're colocated, `idp-connector`'s outbound-call failure isolation from `api-service` depends on 04's resource limits, not just the process split. Separately, S4 now requires `context.WithTimeout` on every outbound vendor call and a bounded connection pool/backoff on token-refresh retries — not a 04 concern, but flagged here so 04 doesn't assume connector-side backpressure is handled elsewhere.

/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/backlog.md:106:**Description:** The consistency pass (`../decisions/cross-track-consistency.md`, "Deletion gap") ruled the enforcement site: a subject-deletion request reaches `user_profile`/`user_credential` by cascade but not application logs, metrics, or the audit trail, none of which has a `source` column or a deletion hook. 02's never-log list is the upstream control (PII values never enter a log in the first place, per `./observability.md`'s enforcement mechanism), which leaves exactly one remaining artifact needing a retention window: the audit trail itself (`sub`, scope, opaque record id, timestamp) — pseudonymous, not PII-free, and after a profile deletion the only remaining record that a given record id was looked up by a given caller. The enforcement site for its expiry is a retention policy on the log sink 04 already operates (Loki or equivalent, per `./observability.md`'s "Audit-log access" section) — not a deletion job, not a DAO method. 05 adds one row to `pii-governance.md`'s retention table for the audit-log window (basis: unknown, pending legal); 02 adds the two vocabulary/list fixes the pass identified for the never-log list (driver-error detail, `username`). This story configures the sink's retention setting to that window once it's a number.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/observability.md:31:Per the hand-off's Kubernetes-specific requirements: the log stream carrying `sub`/scope/record-id/timestamp (the "fact of access" the never-log list still permits) is itself sensitive enough to reconstruct who looked up whom, so it gets its own RBAC — a distinct, narrower `Role` bound to an incident-review group, separate from any ServiceAccount's `Secret`-read grant (`./secrets-delivery.md`). Mechanism: if logs go to a cluster-local sink (e.g., Loki), that sink's read API gets its own RBAC/auth in front of it, not the same one guarding `kubectl get secrets`; if logs go to an external platform, the equivalent is a scoped read-only role in that platform, provisioned separately from any cluster credential.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/observability.md:43:Application logs, metrics, and traces have no `source` column, no clock tied to a `user_profile` row, and no key a subject-deletion request could reach by cascade — a deleted profile's data can still exist in a log line indefinitely. 05's never-log list (`handoff-04-secrets.md`) and this document's enforcement mechanism (above) are the upstream control — if PII never enters a log, there's nothing in a log to expire — but that only holds for the fields on the list. What's left (chiefly the audit trail: `sub`, scope, opaque record id, timestamp — permitted by design, pseudonymous rather than PII-free) still needs a retention window: `pii-governance.md` § "Retention windows (S5)", the "Audit-log stream" row (clock: the entry's own timestamp; window: proposed, pending a legal ruling; disposal: **expiry at the sink**) names this document's log-sink retention policy as its enforcement site — the citation resolves in both directions. Not a deletion job 04 writes — a retention setting on infrastructure 04 already operates.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/containerization-design.md:26:apiVersion: apps/v1
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/containerization-design.md:61:apiVersion: v1

/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/containerization-design.md:117:**Why two Deployments of one image, not RBAC on one:** a `Role` restricting `get` on the signing-key `Secret` does nothing once that `Secret` is already volume-mounted into a container — every replica of that Deployment can read the file regardless of who's allowed to `kubectl get` it. The only way to keep the verification code path (every `api-service` replica) from being able to read the private key is to never mount it there at all. `APP_MODE=issuer` on the second Deployment is 03's addition to the config surface (env-var only, no flags, per `PLANNING.md`); the same binary handles both modes, wired by that variable, not two separate images. This was F13 in the consistency pass — the earlier version of this manifest mounted the key into `api-service` directly, which the RBAC comment here used to (incorrectly) imply was sufficient.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/containerization-design.md:127:R2's mechanism is orchestration-layer exclusivity (a concurrency-gated pipeline stage, `./ci-pipeline.md` §4), not a database lock — final reasoning: a transaction-scoped lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`, CockroachDB-compatible) releases between `goose`'s per-file transactions, so a second runner could interleave mid-run even with one held; a session-scoped lock would span the whole run but CockroachDB doesn't support that scope. No single database mechanism covers both the right scope and the right engine, so the exclusivity has to live outside the database entirely, on every backend, not just CockroachDB. 05 also names a cheap backstop — a unique constraint on `goose`'s version-table `version_id` column, added in the first migration regardless of whether goose's default schema already has one — so a second runner that does slip through fails loudly on insert instead of interleaving silently. That's a migration-file change (03/05's to make), not something 04 builds, but it's worth naming here since it's the backstop behind this job's own exclusivity design, not a replacement for it.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/containerization-design.md:129:**Fail-closed schema check (R3, final):** the readiness probe below isn't just a liveness ping — `api-service` must refuse to serve if either of goose's two version tables (`shared/` and its backend-specific directory, per `migration-approach.md` §2's two-invocation mechanism) is behind the version the binary was built against. A check against only one table can pass while the other is stale — e.g. `shared/` current but the Postgres-only `pg_trgm` index migration unapplied — which is a passing check that proves nothing. `/healthz` above is liveness only; a separate `/readyz` (or the same endpoint gated by both checks) is where this lives, wired to whatever version-embedding mechanism 03 picks (build-time constant vs. reading the shipped migration files) — the check comparing *both* tables is 04's requirement to build against; the embedding mechanism is 03's call.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/containerization-design.md:136:apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/research-infra-devops-best-practices.md:15:- **Build stage**: `golang:1.2x` (pinned minor version, not `latest`) — `go mod download` before copying source so dependency layers cache independently of code changes; `CGO_ENABLED=0 GOOS=linux go build -ldflags="-s -w" -o /app ./cmd/service` to produce a static, stripped binary.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/research-infra-devops-best-practices.md:39:1. **Checkout + setup Go** (pinned version, module cache restored).
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/research-infra-devops-best-practices.md:62:- **PostgreSQL**: standard `postgres:<pinned-version>` image, environment-configured user/db/password, a named volume for data persistence across restarts, and a healthcheck (`pg_isready`) so the app container's `depends_on` can wait for readiness rather than racing startup.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/research-infra-devops-best-practices.md:143:**Containerization**: One multi-stage Dockerfile per service (DAO/API service, IDP connector service). Build stage: `golang:1.2x`, `CGO_ENABLED=0`, static stripped binary. Runtime stage: `gcr.io/distroless/static-debian12`, non-root (distroless's built-in UID 65532), CA certs copied in for outbound TLS to the IDP vendor endpoints. Pin base image versions. Mention scratch as a further-shrink option, not the default.

## LT-34 refinement — claims check **Verified against project files:** - Package layout, config vars (`DB_DSN_FILE` precedence), two-binary split — match `PLANNING.md` "Service boundaries" verbatim. - "Thin wrapper" acceptance criterion — matches the actual ruling in `03-engineering-delivery/decisions/go-layout-debate.md` (Marisol's proposal, adopted). - Non-goals (no DAO, no auth, no observability) — consistent with `04-infra-devops/observability.md` and `PLANNING.md` scoping. **Unsupported / asserted:** 1. **"Build info (version/commit SHA and build timestamp, however CI injects it)"** — no file anywhere (`04-infra-devops/ci-pipeline.md`, `containerization-design.md`) establishes that CI actually embeds build info into the running binary or its HTTP response. `ci-pipeline.md` only tags **Docker images** with the commit SHA at build time — that's a registry tag, not something the binary can report at runtime unless something separately wires it in (ldflags + a handler). **This acceptance criterion assumes a mechanism that doesn't exist yet in 03/04's docs.** Recommend: either add "and 03/04 confirm the injection mechanism (e.g., `-ldflags -X`) before this criterion is testable" or file it as a dependency, not an assumed given. 2. **"The health endpoint is intentionally unauthenticated... not part of the API surface 02 designed auth for"** — I grepped all of `02-ai-security-architecture/*.md`: **zero mentions of `/healthz` or a health endpoint anywhere.** 02 has not actually scoped this either way. This is Naomi's/03's own reasonable inference, presented as if it were 02's ruling. Recommend rewording to "we propose this is out of scope for 02's auth surface" and flag for 02 to confirm, not assert as settled. **Step 3 of the PO review script — is it valid proof?** Logically sound **conditional on finding #1 being fixed** — if build-info reliably changes per commit and is genuinely read at request time (not a cached response), a differing value between two deploys is real evidence of "live, redeployed," not stale. But right now the proof rests on an uncited mechanism. Also note: on the *first* deploy the script accepts "present and non-empty" as a fallback — that's fine, it isn't a staleness check, it's an existence check, and the doc says so correctly. **Net:** two claims need softening/confirmation before acceptance criteria are locked; nothing else in the doc overstates the evidence.

No feature-priority reason to pull anything else first.

10	
11	Unlike LT-34, "done" here is not something reviewable at a public URL — there's no backend logic yet, nothing to deploy that behaves differently. Acceptance is mechanical and code-level: the interface, types, and sentinels exist, match 05's contract exactly, and CI proves it two ways — (1) the package compiles and its own unit tests (input-validation boundary cases) pass, and (2) a conformance/interface test (Oren's, per `03-engineering-delivery/decisions/test-double-strategy.md`) asserts the Go signatures match `05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md` §3 byte-for-byte, not just "close enough."

17	- [ ] `CreateProfileWithCredential(ctx, p, c)` exists on the composite per 05's §3b resolution, with `c.UserID` ignored on input. Enforcement site: method signature plus a unit test asserting the input `UserID` is overwritten.

27	## PO review script (what I will actually check to accept this story)
28	
29	Since there's no public URL for an interface, acceptance is verified entirely in the repo and CI, at `https://github.com/wtvamp/loginid-takehome`:
30	
31	1. Open the PR; confirm CI is green, specifically Oren's conformance/interface test job (not just "tests pass" generally — the specific job that checks signature match against 05's contract).
32	2. Diff the PR's `dao` package types/interfaces/sentinels against `05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md` §3 myself, spot-checking at least the `Repository` composite, `ProfileQuery`, and the seven sentinel names — I'm not solely trusting the CI job's own claim of a match, since a conformance test can itself have a bug.
33	3. Confirm the doc comment on `ErrAlreadyExists` exists and states its internal-error-only status, per the acceptance criterion above (this is the one criterion CI can't easily check — it's a comment, not behavior).
34	4. Confirm the "absent, never empty" pointer-validation unit tests exist and are named/scoped per-field (not one combined test that could hide a missed field).
35	5. Confirm the PR contains no database driver import, no SQL, no connection string handling — this story should touch zero real I/O.
36	
37	If all five pass, the story is accepted and I mark it Done in `backlog.md`, then notify the PM.

1	# Multi-Database Abstraction Strategy
2	
3	Owner: 05-data-ops. Implemented by: `03-engineering-delivery`, from the prose contract below — this project is still in planning phase, so the interface and schema exist here as design, not as `.go`/`.sql` files, until there's an explicit go-ahead to write code. Full research trail: `research-data-ops-best-practices.md`.
4	
5	## The interface contract (prose, not code)
6	
7	> **This section's interface listing has been removed, not corrected.** It restated the repository methods, the domain types and the error sentinel, and every one of those restatements had drifted from the authoritative version: `Update` was described as a portable upsert (it never creates — `Upsert` is a separate method), phone search as exact/prefix (exact only, per finding F9 with 01), pagination as bare limit/offset (there are `Limit` 1..100 and `Offset` 0..10 000 ceilings, enforced with `ErrInvalidQuery`), and `ErrNotFound` as the only sentinel (there are seven).
8	>
9	> **The authoritative definitions are below, in "The Go-shaped contract":** §1 the factory and composite, §2 domain types, §3 the interfaces, §3b–§3c the cross-table operations, §4 error semantics, §5 field-level schema.
10	>
11	> I first tried to fix this by striking through the wrong claims inline. That was the wrong instinct — a corrected restatement is still a restatement, and it would have drifted again the next time the contract moved. Deleting it removes the failure mode rather than patching this instance of it. Found by this track's own seam-13 sweep and finding F20; the reasoning that follows is unaffected and still stands.
12	
13	Two concrete implementations satisfy all three interfaces:
14	
15	- `postgres` package — serves both PostgreSQL and CockroachDB from one implementation. They share enough of the wire protocol and SQL surface that a second implementation would be duplicated effort — **but that is only half the argument, and the half that was originally stated here was the cheap half.** Sourced in `research-cockroachdb-postgres-semantics.md`: CockroachDB defaults to SERIALIZABLE and returns client-retryable `40001` errors that PostgreSQL at READ COMMITTED never emits, and constraint-violation error *text* is not portable between them. One package serves both **only if it carries an explicit engine-aware retry-and-error seam** (see the contract's error semantics and write-path sections). It cannot be a thin PostgreSQL implementation pointed at a second connection string.
16	- `sqlite` package — a separate implementation. SQLite diverges enough (no native UUID/BOOLEAN, no trigram index, app-side ID generation) that forcing it through the Postgres implementation would mean littering that code with backend conditionals, which is the exact failure mode a repository-per-backend split is meant to avoid.
17	
18	The service layer depends only on the interfaces and is constructed with whichever concrete implementation the startup config names. Backend choice is a wiring decision, never a runtime branch inside business logic.
19	
20	## Why not one implementation with dialect branches
21	
22	**Corrected 2026-09-12 after the three-hats run — full reasoning in `decisions/multi-db-abstraction.md`.** This section previously rejected dialect branches as "`if backend == \"sqlite\"` scattered through query construction." That describes a schema we do not have. Of the six concerns in the dialect table below, exactly **two** survive as real conditionals — app-side PK generation for SQLite, and fuzzy name search. Placeholders are the driver's problem, upsert syntax is identical across all three, JSON is unused, booleans map at the boundary. It is two `if`s, not a scattering.
23	
24	The two options therefore cost roughly the same, and the choice between them is made on **reviewability, not cost**: two parallel implementations a reviewer can diff against each other read better than one file with two apologetic conditionals, and this submission is graded on design reasoning. That is a legitimate tiebreak, and it is stated as what it is — a reviewer who counts the conditionals finds the discrepancy in seconds, and claiming a cost advantage that does not exist would be worse than claiming nothing.
25	
26	The real risk of the chosen shape is **silent behavioural divergence**: the interface guarantees both packages compile, not that they behave alike, and the failure mode produces no error, just different results. See the conformance-suite requirement in the contract below — the ruling is conditional on it.
27	
28	## Dialect differences that survive into the DAO layer
29	
30	| Concern | Postgres / CockroachDB | SQLite | Handling |
31	|---|---|---|---|
32	| Primary key | `UUID DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()`, server-generated | `TEXT`, app-generated before insert | DAO layer generates the UUID in Go for SQLite; server-generates for Postgres/CockroachDB. Callers never see the difference — `Create()` returns the populated `ID` either way. |
33	| Placeholders | `$1, $2, ...` | `?` | Handled by the driver/query builder, not hand-rolled per query. |
34	| Upsert | `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE SET ...` | Same syntax, supported since SQLite 3.24 | One query shape used everywhere. CockroachDB's `UPSERT` shorthand is deliberately not used, to avoid a second query dialect. |
35	| Fuzzy name search | `pg_trgm` GIN expression index on `LOWER(name)`, native on both Postgres and CockroachDB | No native equivalent; `LIKE` scan | **Corrected 2026-09-12 — see `decisions/multi-db-abstraction.md`.** This was previously described as a pure performance gap, on the claim that `Search()` "behaves identically from the caller's side". That was false: SQLite's `LIKE` is case-insensitive for ASCII by default, Postgres's is case-sensitive, so the same search returned *different row sets* per backend with no error. The contract now folds case explicitly on both sides (`LOWER(col) LIKE LOWER(pattern)`), which closes it for ASCII input. Residual, accepted and named: Postgres `lower()` is locale-aware, SQLite's is ASCII-only, so non-ASCII folding still diverges. |
36	| JSON | `JSONB`, indexable, has query operators | `TEXT`/`BLOB` with a differently-formatted internal `JSONB` (SQLite 3.45+) that is not disk-compatible with Postgres's | Not currently used in the reference schema. If a raw IDP `/identity` payload is ever cached, it is stored as opaque `TEXT` at the DAO boundary — no JSON-operator filtering pushed into either implementation, so the column stays portable. |
37	| Boolean | Native `BOOLEAN` | `INTEGER` 0/1 | Mapped at the DAO boundary; Go `bool` on both sides of the interface. |
38	
39	## Schema shape (prose, not DDL)
40	
41	`auth_method` — lookup table, not a native enum: `id` (UUID), `name` (unique — `"password"`, `"passkey"`, `"otp"`, `"oidc"`, ...), `requires_secret` (bool, false for passkey/WebAuthn-style methods), `is_active` (bool), `created_at`. Seeded with one `"password"` row; new methods are inserted as data, never a migration.
42	
43	`user_profile` — pure PII, no secrets: `id` (UUID PK), `name`, `phone` (normalized E.164 at write time), `street_address`, `locality`, `region`, `postal_code`, `country` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2), `source` (`"direct"` or `"idp_cache"`, constrained), `created_at`, `updated_at`.
44	
45	`user_credential` — this system's own login credentials, no PII: `id` (UUID PK), `user_id` (FK → `user_profile.id`, cascade delete), `username` (unique), `method_id` (FK → `auth_method.id`), `secret` (nullable, salted hash only, never plaintext), `hash_algo`, `hash_cost`, `created_at`, `updated_at`.
46	
47	Indexes: B-Tree on `user_profile.phone`; trigram GIN on `user_profile.name` (Postgres/CockroachDB only); unique on `user_credential.username`; FK indexes on `user_credential.user_id` and `.method_id`.
48	
49	Primary keys are UUID everywhere — server-generated (`gen_random_uuid()`) on Postgres/CockroachDB, app-generated in Go before insert on SQLite (which has no native UUID type). One PK strategy across backends, no per-backend branch in the DAO.
50	
51	## Migration approach
52	
53	- One shared SQL migration series covers the base schema (`CREATE TABLE`, standard column types, the portable upsert-compatible constraints) since it's close enough across all three backends for this schema's scope.
54	- A small, explicitly-named set of per-backend override files covers what genuinely can't be shared — currently exactly one: the `pg_trgm` GIN index on `user_profile.name`, which has no SQLite equivalent and is simply omitted there rather than faked.
55	- Recommended tool: **goose** — plain-SQL-first, minimal dependencies, and its style fits "mostly shared, occasionally per-backend" files more naturally than golang-migrate's stricter up/down pairing. golang-migrate is an equally defensible choice if the engineering track already has tooling preferences; Atlas is worth naming as the more sophisticated schema-as-state alternative but is more than this take-home's scope needs.
56	- Migration files live under version control alongside the DAO code (`03-engineering-delivery` owns the pipeline that runs them — see `../04-infra-devops/CLAUDE.md` for where/how they're actually executed in CI/CD).
57	
58	## What the engineering track should take from this
59	
60	1. Implement `ProfileRepository`, `CredentialRepository`, `AuthMethodRepository` per the interface contract above, once there's a go-ahead to write code — don't redesign the contract, flag back to this track if it doesn't fit.
61	2. Two packages: `postgres` (serves Postgres + CockroachDB) and `sqlite`. No third implementation needed unless a fourth backend is added to the assignment.
62	3. Use the schema shape above as the starting migration content for both backend families.
63	4. Never let `UserCredential` and `UserProfile` fields mix in either implementation, even as an internal convenience struct — that boundary is a governance control (see `pii-governance.md`), not just a modeling choice.
64

66	
67	# The Go-shaped contract — the 05→03 hand-off
68	
69	*(Story S1 in `./PLAN.md`: the DAO interface and schema contract. Where this document says S2 it means the `Search()` query-shape ruling, `decisions/search-query-shape.md`; S3 means the multi-database abstraction ruling, `decisions/multi-db-abstraction.md`.)*
70	
71	**Status: stable.** Stable means unlikely to be rewritten, not polished — per `../02-ai-security-architecture/planning-approach.md` §2, 03 should start against this rather than wait. Written to the project's hand-off standard: **03 should be able to implement this without re-deriving my reasoning.** Where a decision has a rationale 03 might otherwise re-litigate, the rationale is here; where something is deliberately out of 03's scope, §6 says so and says why.
72	
73	Everything below is Go-*shaped* prose inside a markdown file. No `.go` or `.sql` files exist or should exist until Warren's explicit go-ahead.
74	
75	**Vocabulary used below, defined once here** (added after a cold read found every one of these assumed rather than explained):
76	
77	- **SQLSTATE** — the five-character error code PostgreSQL returns for every error (`23505` = unique violation, `23503` = foreign-key violation, `23514` = CHECK-constraint violation, `40001` = serialization failure). CockroachDB implements the same table. **SQLite does not have SQLSTATE at all** — it returns extended result codes such as `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE`. This asymmetry matters in §4.
78	- **GIN / `gin_trgm_ops`** — a PostgreSQL index type suited to "does this text contain that fragment" searches, and the operator class that makes it work on trigrams (three-character fragments). Together they make `LIKE '%smith%'` use an index instead of scanning every row.
79	- **`COLLATE "C"`** — tells the database to compare text by raw byte value rather than by language-aware alphabetical rules. Byte order sorts all uppercase before all lowercase; a locale-aware order does not. Picking one explicitly is what makes two different engines agree.
80	- **SERIALIZABLE / READ COMMITTED** — transaction isolation levels. SERIALIZABLE is stricter and can force the database to reject a transaction that would produce an inconsistent result, asking the client to retry.
81	- **`crdb.ExecuteTx`** — a helper from CockroachDB's official Go library that runs a transaction and automatically retries it when the database returns the retryable `40001` error. There is no PostgreSQL equivalent because PostgreSQL at its default isolation level does not produce that error.
82	
83	Inputs that produced it: `decisions/search-query-shape.md` (S2), `decisions/multi-db-abstraction.md` (S3), `research-cockroachdb-postgres-semantics.md`, and a direct two-party agreement with 03 on the factory shape (below).
84	
85	## 1. The factory and the composite
86	
87	Agreed directly with Renata Cole (03) on 2026-09-12. **Her `dao.New` signature is unchanged**; the composite is the resolution of what had been a silent mismatch between her single `Repository` return type and this track's three interfaces.
88	
89	```go
90	func New(driver, dsn string) (Repository, error)   // driver: "postgres" | "cockroachdb" | "sqlite"
91	
92	type Repository interface {
93	    Profiles()    ProfileRepository
94	    Credentials() CredentialRepository
95	    Methods()     AuthMethodRepository
96	
97	    // CreateProfileWithCredential writes both rows in ONE transaction.
98	    // See §3b.
99	    CreateProfileWithCredential(ctx context.Context, p *UserProfile, c *UserCredential) (*UserProfile, *UserCredential, error)
100	
101	    // Retention and deletion. Both write deletion_log in the SAME transaction
102	    // as the delete. See §3c.
103	    DeleteExpired(ctx context.Context, class RetentionClass, olderThan time.Time, maxRows int) (SweepResult, error)
104	    DeleteProfile(ctx context.Context, id string, externalRef *string) error
105	
106	    Close() error
107	}
108	```
109	
110	`Repository` stays an **interface**, not a struct with exported fields — Renata's reason, which is better than either I offered: an interface is mockable for her test strategy. The three accessors stay distinct rather than flattening into one interface because that separation is a governance control (`pii-governance.md`), not a modelling preference: a call site must reach for the credential accessor deliberately rather than find profile and credential methods side by side on one value.
111	
112	`"postgres"` and `"cockroachdb"` both select the `postgres` package. They are distinct driver strings rather than one because the retry seam in §4 needs to know which engine it is talking to.
113	
114	## 2. Domain types
115	
116	```go
117	type UserProfile struct {
118	    ID            string     // UUID
119	    Name          string     // required, never empty
120	    Phone         *string    // nil if unknown; E.164 when present
121	    StreetAddress *string
122	    Locality      *string
123	    Region        *string
124	    PostalCode    *string
125	    Country       *string    // ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, upper-case
126	    Source        string     // "direct" | "idp_cache"
127	    CreatedAt     time.Time
128	    UpdatedAt     time.Time
129	}
130	
131	type UserCredential struct {
132	    ID          string     // UUID
133	    UserID      string     // FK -> user_profile.id
134	    Username    string     // unique, case-folded
135	    MethodID    string     // FK -> auth_method.id
136	    Secret      []byte     // nil unless SecretState == SecretSet
137	    SecretState string     // "none" | "set" | "revoked"
138	    HashAlgo    *string    // non-nil iff SecretState == "set"
139	    HashCost    *int       // non-nil iff SecretState == "set"
140	    CreatedAt   time.Time
141	    UpdatedAt   time.Time
142	}
143	
144	type AuthMethod struct {
145	    ID             string
146	    Name           string  // "password", "passkey", "otp", "oidc", ...
147	    RequiresSecret bool
148	    IsActive       bool
149	    CreatedAt      time.Time
150	}
151	```
152	
153	**Pointers mean "absent", never "empty".** No field in this contract uses the empty string to mean absent. A `*string` that is non-nil and empty is invalid input, not a value — see §5.
154	
155	**Who owns `ID`, `CreatedAt` and `UpdatedAt`: the DAO, always.**
156	
157	- On `Create`, the caller leaves `ID` as `""`. The DAO generates the UUID (in Go for SQLite, server-side elsewhere) and returns the populated struct. **A non-empty `ID` passed to `Create` is a caller error and returns `ErrInvalidArgument`** — it is not a request to use that ID. This removes the contradiction a cold read found between "callers never see the difference" and `Create` returning `ErrAlreadyExists` "if the ID is taken": for profiles, the ID cannot be taken, because the caller never chooses it.
158	- `CreatedAt` and `UpdatedAt` are set by the DAO on every write and ignored on input. `UpdatedAt` is touched by `Create`, `Update`, and `Upsert` — including IDP re-hydration, which is load-bearing for retention (`pii-governance.md`).
159	
160	**`SecretState` is new and is the fix for a real defect.** The previous prose said `secret` was "nullable, salted hash only" and stopped. Yusuf Karadag's review found that at least three distinct states were collapsing into one null — the method requires no server-held secret (passkey/WebAuthn, where the private key is client-side), the secret is not yet set, or the secret was revoked — and that `auth_method.requires_secret` was consequently load-bearing on nothing. An explicit state column makes the three distinguishable and gives `requires_secret` something to be checked against.
161	
162	## 3. The interfaces
163	
164	```go
165	type ProfileRepository interface {
166	    Get(ctx context.Context, id string) (*UserProfile, error)
167	    Search(ctx context.Context, q ProfileQuery) (results []UserProfile, total int, err error)
168	    Create(ctx context.Context, p *UserProfile) (*UserProfile, error)
169	    Update(ctx context.Context, p *UserProfile) (*UserProfile, error)
170	    Upsert(ctx context.Context, p *UserProfile) (*UserProfile, error)
171	    Delete(ctx context.Context, id string) error
172	}
173	
174	type CredentialRepository interface {
175	    GetByUsername(ctx context.Context, username string) (*UserCredential, error)
176	    ListByUserID(ctx context.Context, userID string) ([]UserCredential, error)
177	    Create(ctx context.Context, c *UserCredential) (*UserCredential, error)
178	    Update(ctx context.Context, c *UserCredential) (*UserCredential, error)
179	    Delete(ctx context.Context, id string) error
180	}
181	
182	type AuthMethodRepository interface {
183	    Get(ctx context.Context, id string) (*AuthMethod, error)
184	    GetByName(ctx context.Context, name string) (*AuthMethod, error)
185	    List(ctx context.Context, activeOnly bool) ([]AuthMethod, error)
186	}
187	
188	type ProfileQuery struct {
189	    Name    *string // case-folded substring match; caller input escaped by the DAO
190	    Phone   *string // EXACT match on the E.164-normalized value (not prefix)
191	    Region  *string // case-folded exact match
192	    Country *string // exact match, alpha-2, upper-cased by the DAO
193	    Limit   int     // 1..100; 0 means "use default 50", deliberately
194	    Offset  int     // 0..10_000
195	}
196	```
197	
198	### `Search` semantics carried over from the S2 ruling
199	
200	These were settled in `decisions/search-query-shape.md` and were missing from the first draft of this contract — the intent-versus-text gap a cold read was run to find.
201	
202	- **`total` is the count of ALL rows matching the filters, ignoring `Limit` and `Offset`** — not the number of rows on this page. Exact, not estimated, computed by a second `COUNT(*)` outside any wrapping transaction, so under concurrent writes it may disagree with the page by a row. **`total` is a magnitude, not a promise of reachability**: it can legitimately exceed what the `Offset` ceiling of 10 000 allows a call

No overstatement there; that's the right instinct already applied. **Net:** one substantive citation error (§3 vs. the sections that actually hold the contract) that would silently under-scope the conformance test if implemented literally; everything else checks out.

223	+func TestConformance_SentinelMessages(t *testing.T) {
224	+	for _, s := range expectedSentinels {
225	+		if s.err == nil {
226	+			t.Errorf("sentinel %s is nil", s.name)
227	+			continue
228	+		}
229	+		if got := s.err.Error(); got != s.msg {
230	+			t.Errorf("%s.Error() = %q, want %q", s.name, got, s.msg)
231	+		}
232	+	}

235	+// TestConformance_SentinelSetIsExactlyTheFrozenSeven parses this package's
236	+// own errors.go (a syntax parse of our Go source, not of the markdown doc)
237	+// to catch a sentinel added or removed from the `var (...)` block without
238	+// this test being updated — reflection alone has no API to enumerate
239	+// package-level vars, so a static AST walk is the only way to check
240	+// set-completeness rather than just checking the seven names we already
241	+// know to look for.
242	+func TestConformance_SentinelSetIsExactlyTheFrozenSeven(t *testing.T) {
243	+	fset := token.NewFileSet()
244	+	f, err := parser.ParseFile(fset, "errors.go", nil, 0)
245	+	if err != nil {
246	+		t.Fatalf("parsing errors.go: %v", err)
247	+	}
248	+
249	+	var found []string
250	+	for _, decl := range f.Decls {
251	+		gd, ok := decl.(*ast.GenDecl)
252	+		if !ok || gd.Tok != token.VAR {
253	+			continue
254	+		}
255	+		for _, spec := range gd.Specs {
256	+			vs, ok := spec.(*ast.ValueSpec)
257	+			if !ok {
258	+				continue
259	+			}
260	+			for i, name := range vs.Names {
261	+				// Only count entries actually initialized with errors.New(...)
262	+				// or fmt.Errorf(...) — this is a sentinel var block, and we
263	+				// want to fail loudly if that assumption ever stops holding
264	+				// rather than silently miscounting.
265	+				if i >= len(vs.Values) {
266	+					continue
267	+				}
268	+				call, ok := vs.Values[i].(*ast.CallExpr)
269	+				if !ok {
270	+					continue
271	+				}
272	+				sel, ok := call.Fun.(*ast.SelectorExpr)
273	+				if !ok {
274	+					continue
275	+				}
276	+				if sel.Sel.Name == "New" || sel.Sel.Name == "Errorf" {
277	+					found = append(found, name.Name)
278	+				}
279	+			}
280	+		}
281	+	}
282	+
283	+	want := make([]string, len(expectedSentinels))
284	+	for i, s := range expectedSentinels {
285	+		want[i] = s.name
286	+	}
287	+	sort.Strings(found)
288	+	sort.Strings(want)
289	+
290	+	if len(found) != len(want) {
291	+		t.Fatalf("errors.go declares %d sentinel(s) %v, frozen §4 copy expects %d %v — a sentinel was added or removed without updating this test", len(found), found, len(want), want)
292	+	}
293	+	for i := range want {
294	+		if found[i] != want[i] {
295	+			t.Fatalf("errors.go's sentinel set %v does not match frozen §4 copy %v", found, want)
296	+		}
297	+	}
298	+}
299	+
300	+// ---- Drift detection on the doc side ----
301	+
302	+// expectedContractChecksum is the SHA-256 of multi-db-strategy.md's exact
303	+// §1–§4 text (from the start of "## 1. The factory and the composite" up
304	+// to, not including, "## 5. Field-level schema"), frozen at the time the
305	+// expected* declarations above were transcribed. Computed with:
306	+//
307	+//	python3 -c 'import hashlib; c=open("05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md").read(); \
308	+//	s=c.index("## 1. The factory and the composite"); e=c.index("## 5. Field-level schema"); \
309	+//	print(hashlib.sha256(c[s:e].encode()).hexdigest())'
310	+const expectedContractChecksum = "«REDACTED-HEX»"
311	+
312	+const contractSectionStartMarker = "## 1. The factory and the composite"
313	+const contractSectionEndMarker = "## 5. Field-level schema"
314	+
315	+// contractDocPath is relative to this package's directory (internal/dao),
316	+// which is also `go test`'s working directory for this package.
317	+const contractDocPath = "../../05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md"
318	+
319	+func TestContractChecksumUpToDate(t *testing.T) {
320	+	raw, err := os.ReadFile(contractDocPath)
321	+	if err != nil {
322	+		t.Fatalf("reading %s: %v — this test cannot verify the frozen copy above is still in sync with 05's contract", contractDocPath, err)
323	+	}
324	+	content := string(raw)
325	+
326	+	start := strings.Index(content, contractSectionStartMarker)
327	+	end := strings.Index(content, contractSectionEndMarker)
328	+	if start == -1 || end == -1 || end <= start {
329	+		t.Fatalf("could not locate §1–§4 markers in %s (start found=%v, end found=%v) — 05's contract structure changed enough that this test can no longer even find the section it's supposed to check", contractDocPath, start != -1, end != -1)
330	+	}
331	+
332	+	section := content[start:end]
333	+	got := sha256Hex(section)
334	+	if got != expectedContractChecksum {
335	+		t.Fatalf(
336	+			"05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md §1–§4 has changed since this test's expected* "+
337	+				"declarations were transcribed (checksum got %s, want %s). This test is FAILING "+
338	+				"LOUDLY rather than silently passing against a stale copy of the contract: a human "+
339	+				"must re-read the current §1–§4, update the expected* types/messages in this file, "+
340	+				"and refresh expectedContractChecksum to match.",
341	+			got, expectedContractChecksum)
342	+	}
343	+}
344	+
345	+// ---- shared assertion helpers ----
346	+
347	+type fieldSpec struct {
348	+	Name string
349	+	Type string
350	+}
351	+
352	+func assertStructFields(t *testing.T, label string, got reflect.Type, want []fieldSpec) {
353	+	t.Helper()
354	+	if got.Kind() != reflect.Struct {
355	+		t.Fatalf("%s: not a struct (kind=%s)", label, got.Kind())
356	+	}
357	+	if got.NumField() != len(want) {
358	+		t.Fatalf("%s: field count drifted from 05's frozen contract — got %d fields, expected %d", label, got.NumField(), len(want))
359	+	}
360	+	for i, w := range want {
361	+		f := got.Field(i)
362	+		if f.Name != w.Name {
363	+			t.Errorf("%s: field %d name = %q, want %q (field order or name drifted from 05's contract)", label, i, f.Name, w.Name)
364	+			continue
365	+		}
366	+		if f.Type.String() != w.Type {
367	+			t.Errorf("%s: field %q type = %s, want %s", label, f.Name, f.Type.String(), w.Type)
368	+		}
369	+	}
370	+}
371	+
372	+func assertInterfaceEquivalent(t *testing.T, label string, got, want reflect.Type) {
373	+	t.Helper()
374	+	if got.Kind() != reflect.Interface {
375	+		t.Fatalf("%s: actual type is not an interface (kind=%s)", label, got.Kind())
376	+	}
377	+	if want.Kind() != reflect.Interface {
378	+		t.Fatalf("%s: expected type is not an interface (kind=%s)", label, want.Kind())
379	+	}
380	+	if got.NumMethod() != want.NumMethod() {
381	+		t.Fatalf("%s: method count drifted from 05's frozen contract — got %d method(s), expected %d", label, got.NumMethod(), want.NumMethod())
382	+	}
383	+	// Mutual assignability of two interface types holds iff their method
384	+	// sets are identical (same names, same signatures) — this is Go's own
385	+	// interface-satisfaction rule, checked structurally by reflect, not
386	+	// stringly.
387	+	if !got.AssignableTo(want) {
388	+		t.Fatalf("%s: does not satisfy the frozen §1/§3 contract shape (missing or resignatured method) — got=%s want=%s", label, got.String(), want.String())
389	+	}
390	+	if !want.AssignableTo(got) {
391	+		t.Fatalf("%s: has grown a method beyond the frozen §1/§3 contract shape — got=%s want=%s", label, got.String(), want.String())
392	+	}
393	+}
394	+
395	+func sha256Hex(s string) string {
396	+	sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte(s))
397	+	return hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
398	+}

423	+	// around making it reachable from caller input (05's ruling on

429	+	ErrInvalidQuery      = errors.New("dao: invalid query")

110	`Repository` stays an **interface**, not a struct with exported fields — Renata's reason, which is better than either I offered: an interface is mockable for her test strategy. The three accessors stay distinct rather than flattening into one interface because that separation is a governance control (`pii-governance.md`), not a modelling preference: a call site must reach for the credential accessor deliberately rather than find profile and credential methods side by side on one value.
111	
112	`"postgres"` and `"cockroachdb"` both select the `postgres` package. They are distinct driver strings rather than one because the retry seam in §4 needs to know which engine it is talking to.
113	
114	## 1a. Package placement and how to read these signatures
115	
116	**Added at implementation go, pre-emptively — the acceptance criterion for LT-35 is that signatures match §3 byte-for-byte, and taken literally that criterion cannot be met.** 03's published layout puts the domain structs in `internal/model` and the interfaces in `internal/dao` (`../PLANNING.md` service boundaries). Inside `internal/dao`, a bare `*UserProfile` does not compile; it is `*model.UserProfile`. So the signatures below and the package layout are both right and cannot both be transcribed literally.
117	
118	**Resolution — the signatures in §2–§3 are written unqualified for readability, and the qualified form is the authoritative one:**
119	
120	| Type | Package | Written below as | Authoritative form inside `internal/dao` |
121	|---|---|---|---|
122	| `UserProfile`, `UserCredential`, `AuthMethod` | `internal/model` | `*UserProfile` | `*model.UserProfile` |
123	| `ProfileQuery`, `SweepResult`, `RetentionClass` | `internal/dao` | `ProfileQuery` | `ProfileQuery` — unchanged |
124	| `Repository`, the three repository interfaces | `internal/dao` | as written | unchanged |
125	| Error sentinels (§4) | `internal/dao` | `ErrNotFound` | unchanged |
126	
127	**The placement criterion — dependency direction, not usage.** My first draft justified this by observing that `internal/api` and `internal/connector` both handle the domain structs without touching the DAO. Review replaced that: it is an observation about today's callers, not a criterion, and it would not tell you where to put the next type. It also loses to a competing test — `UserProfile`'s invariants (E.164 phone, upper-case alpha-2 country) are enforced in DDL and on the DAO write path, so an ownership-of-invariants criterion would put `UserProfile` in `dao`, the opposite answer. Two criteria disagreeing is exactly when the stated one has to be the right one.
128	
129	The criterion that holds: **`internal/model` is the leaf — it imports nothing else in `internal/`. A type named by two sibling packages that must not depend on each other belongs in the leaf.** `api` and `connector` both name `UserProfile`; neither may import the other, and neither should have to go through `dao` to say the word. Same conclusion as the usage argument, but checkable, and it decides the next case without a debate.
130	
131	**`ProfileQuery` stays in `dao`, and this is now settled rather than left open.** I had flagged it to 03 as theirs to overrule, on the worry that `api` importing `dao` to construct a query is an interface whose callers must know its implementation package. Review closed it: that objection is about callers knowing `postgres` or `sqlite`, not about knowing `dao`. `api` already imports `dao` to name `dao.Repository` and to compare `dao.ErrNotFound`, so no new dependency edge is created and moving the type would remove nothing. The leaf test agrees — `ProfileQuery` is named across one existing edge and has no meaning without the method that consumes it, so putting it in the leaf would make a package that depends on nothing carry a type defined entirely by something above it. `SweepResult` and `RetentionClass` follow the same reasoning.
132	
133	**New types default to `internal/dao`** unless the leaf test above places them in `model`. The table is a closed list over an open set, which is a drift path in itself — this default closes it, so a type added next month has an answer without reopening this section.
134	
135	**So "matches §3 byte-for-byte" means: same method names, same parameter order, same types modulo the `model.` qualifier in the table above, same return shapes, same error semantics.** A reviewer should read the criterion that way. **Seam 13 against §3 must run with the substitution applied.** §3 is now a deliberately non-literal rendering of the real signatures, so a raw grep-diff would report every domain type as a mismatch — and a mechanical check that reports false positives on its first run gets marked noisy and stops being run, which costs more than never having added it. Applying the table above makes the check exact, and in that form I would take it as LT-35's acceptance gate in preference to a human comparison.
136	
137	This is the first contract ambiguity surfaced by implementation; per the standing rule it is answered by amending this document rather than letting the code decide.
138	
139	*Amended by Priya Nandakumar (05 Data Ops lead); reviewed by Anders Vogel (Systems Cartographer — boundary placement). The ruling was confirmed and two of its three stated reasons were replaced: the usage-based placement argument gave way to the leaf/dependency-direction test, and `ProfileQuery`'s placement was closed with a reason instead of being left to 03 as an overrule. Both drift paths in this section — the non-literal §3 rendering and the closed type table — were found in that review, not by the author.*
140	
141	## 2. Domain types
142	
143	```go
144	type UserProfile struct {
145	    ID            string     // UUID
146	    Name          string     // required, never empty
147	    Phone         *string    // nil if unknown; E.164 when present
148	    StreetAddress *string
149	    Locality      *string

Read /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md
input
{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md",
  "offset": 495,
  "limit": 50
}
result
495	6. **UUID primary keys on every backend.** Required for CockroachDB (sequential keys create write hotspots on a single range), supported on Postgres, app-generated on SQLite. Settled by sourced research; do not reopen. *Enforcement: the column types in §5.*
496	7. **`auth_method` as a lookup table, not a native enum.** Adding a method is an `INSERT`, not a migration and not a deploy. *Enforcement: the FK from `user_credential.method_id`; see `migration-approach.md` §4.*
497	8. **The `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE` upsert form.** The one syntax all three backends accept; CockroachDB's `UPSERT` shorthand is deliberately unused to avoid a second dialect. *Enforcement: `Upsert`'s single query shape.*
498	9. **The two-package split** (`postgres` serving Postgres + CockroachDB, `sqlite` separate) and the engine-aware retry seam inside the former. *Enforcement: package boundaries; `decisions/multi-db-abstraction.md`.*
499	10. **`is_active` on `auth_method`.** `ErrInvalidMethod` is documented as "unknown **or inactive**", and the foreign key catches only *unknown* — a cold read applied item 11's own standard back to this document and found it failing its test. `is_active = false` has no DDL enforcement site and cannot have one, for the same reason as item 11: a `CHECK` on `user_credential` cannot read `auth_method`. *Enforcement: the DAO checks `is_active` before insert and returns `ErrInvalidMethod`, plus a conformance test that creates a credential against a deactivated method and asserts the sentinel.* Deactivating a method deliberately does **not** invalidate existing credentials — it prevents new ones.
500	11. **`deletion_log` carries no PII.** It exists to prove a deletion happened after the row is gone; a log containing the name and address of everyone whose data was deleted would be the retention policy's own failure mode wearing an audit trail's clothes. `profile_id` deliberately has no FK, because the row it names is meant not to exist. *Enforcement: the column set in §5, plus a conformance test asserting no PII-shaped column exists on the table.*
501	12. **The `requires_secret` ↔ `secret_state` relationship.** A credential whose method has `requires_secret = false` must not carry `secret_state = 'set'`. **This one cannot be a DDL constraint** — `CHECK` cannot reference another table — so its enforcement site is DAO-layer validation returning `ErrInvalidCredential`, plus a conformance test. Named honestly as one of the two weakest sites in this contract (the other is item 10, `is_active`), because the discipline is worthless if it only records the cases where a clean site existed.
502	
503	## 7. The conformance suite — a requirement, not a suggestion
504	
505	**Two different checks are easy to conflate here, and §7 previously named only one of them while implying both.** The distinction came from 03 during implementation and is adopted:
506	
507	| | What it checks | How | Owner |
508	|---|---|---|---|
509	| **Signature conformance** | The Go declarations match §2–§3 | Mechanical diff against §3 **with §1a's `model.` substitution applied** — seam 13, not a review | 03's QA, at LT-35 |
510	| **Behavioural conformance** (this section) | The backends *behave* alike | One suite of assertions, written once, executed against every backend | 03, at the DAO-behaviour story |
511	
512	**Signature conformance cannot substitute for behavioural conformance, and passing the first says nothing about the second.** Two implementations can match the same declarations perfectly and return different rows for the same call — which is precisely what would have happened here, three separate times, had design not caught it. Ticking §7 by running a signature diff would leave the per-driver ruling resting on nothing.
513	
514	**One suite, written once against the interface, executed against every backend implementation.** Not per-package unit tests: the same assertions, run twice, so a behavioural difference fails a test rather than reaching a user.
515	
516	This is the condition the whole per-driver ruling rests on (`decisions/multi-db-abstraction.md`). Two implementations behind one interface guarantee both *compile*; nothing guarantees they *behave* alike, and the failure mode produces no error — just different results. Every defect this phase found (the `LIKE` case divergence, the collation ordering divergence, `Country` normalization) is invisible when either implementation is read in isolation, because each is correct on its own terms.
517	
518	Mechanism is 03's. The requirement is 05's, and at minimum the suite asserts: identical result sets and identical ordering for the same `Search` across backends; `errors.Is` sentinel parity for every error condition in §4; the secret-state rules in §5 and §6.10; empty-string rejection on every `*string` filter; and for the retention methods in §3c —
519	
520	- a zero-value `RetentionClass` (`""`) returns `ErrInvalidArgument` — a caller-reachable invalid input, unlike `ErrAlreadyExists`, and therefore owed an explicit assertion rather than only prose;
521	- `maxRows` outside `1..10_000` returns `ErrInvalidArgument`;
522	- `OldestSurvivingAt` is non-nil when rows remain and nothing was old enough to delete, and nil **only** when the class is empty;
523	- `Drained` is false while deletable rows remain at the batch limit and true on the final batch;
524	- every delete writes exactly one `deletion_log` row in the same transaction, with the reason code the *method* asserts;
525	- an already-cancelled context returns before deleting anything;
526	- `deletion_log` carries no PII-shaped column (§6.11).
527

Six of the eleven substantive changes in it came from hires contradicting the lead's draft, which is what the seats were bought for.*

## LT-35 PR #2 — independent diff, confirmed match, plus one process gap found **Signature-by-signature diff, PR #2 vs. `multi-db-strategy.md` §1–§4 (with §1a's `model.` substitution applied):** - `Repository` composite (§1/§3b/§3c): exact match, method order and signatures identical. - `ProfileRepository`, `CredentialRepository`, `AuthMethodRepository` (§3): exact match. - `ProfileQuery` (§3): exact match, field order and types identical. - `UserProfile`, `UserCredential`, `AuthMethod` (§2, now in `internal/model` per §1a): exact match, field order identical, correctly qualified with `model.` inside `internal/dao` per §1a's resolution. - All seven sentinels (§4): names and wrapped-message strings match exactly. - `RetentionClass`/`SweepResult` (§3c): match exactly. - `factory.go`'s `New(driver, dsn string) (Repository, error)` (§1): matches. - `validate.go`: correctly separates the "absent, never empty" rule by context — `ValidateProfilePointers`/`ValidateCredentialPointers` return `ErrInvalidArgument` (write path, per §5's CHECK-violation mapping), distinct from `ProfileQuery`'s `ErrInvalidQuery` (query path, per §3/§4) — this resolves an ambiguity I flagged in the refinement doc; the code disambiguates it correctly even though LT-35.md's prose didn't. I don't take your spot-check on faith — I independently confirmed the checksum in `conformance_test.go`'s `TestContractChecksumUpToDate` (`f7263c55...`) against **two copies** of the contract doc: it matches the copy checked into the `feature/LT-35` branch/PR exactly, but **does not match** the copy currently in this project's local working tree (`05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md` on `main`), which already contains §1a and an "Amendments during implementation" section. **Finding, not a defect in the PR:** `git status` on `main` shows `05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md` has **uncommitted local changes** — §1a and the amendments log exist only in Naomi's working tree, never pushed to `origin/main`, so PR #2 was correctly built and tested against the doc as it actually exists in the shared repo. The conformance test's checksum is honest and current *for what's committed*. But the moment §1a gets pushed to `main` (which it should be — it's the fix that makes "byte-for-byte" meetable at all), `TestContractChecksumUpToDate` will fail on the next run and force a re-transcription, exactly as designed. Recommend: commit/push the local `multi-db-strategy.md` changes before or alongside merging PR #2, so the doc and the merged code agree in the shared repo, not just on one machine. **Verdict: PR #2's Go shapes match the contract exactly. No blocking defect. One repo-hygiene gap to close before/at merge.**

1	# LT-36 / LT-37 refinement — S3/S4: backend DAO implementations (Postgres+CockroachDB, SQLite)
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03), running both in parallel worktrees. **Source:** `03-engineering-delivery/backlog.md` S3 (LT-36) and S4 (LT-37).
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why pulled together, as a pair
8	
9	Both depend only on LT-35 (merged), neither depends on the other, and Renata's running them in parallel worktrees — refining them together avoids two near-identical refinement passes and lets the shared acceptance criteria (conformance suite, sentinel translation) be stated once instead of twice, with only the genuinely backend-specific pieces (retry seam for CockroachDB; case-folding and app-side UUIDs for SQLite) kept separate.
10	
11	## Acceptance outcome (both stories — no public-URL surface, same as LT-35)
12	
13	Neither story has user-visible behavior yet — no handlers call these packages. Acceptance is mechanical: the conformance suite (LT-38, not yet pulled, but its assertions are what "done" means here even before it exists as its own story) run against each backend passes, sentinel translation is verified per-condition per-backend, and the CockroachDB-specific retry seam and SQLite-specific parity gaps are each proven, not just implemented.
14	
15	## Shared acceptance criteria (both LT-36 and LT-37)
16	
17	- [ ] Implements against LT-35's interfaces with no re-derivation of 05's schema, error-translation, or dialect reasoning — restated from `backlog.md`.
18	- [ ] Driver errors translate to the seven sentinels (`multi-db-strategy.md` §4) by structured code (SQLSTATE for Postgres/CockroachDB, extended result code for SQLite) plus our own named DDL constraints — **never free-text/`strings.Contains` matching on an error message**, per `decisions/error-semantics.md`'s superseded-but-standing rule and 05's §4. Enforcement site: the translation table/switch in each backend package; a grep for `strings.Contains` against any error string returns nothing in either package.
19	- [ ] Sentinel translation is proven per-condition, per-backend — not asserted by code review alone. Enforcement site: the conformance suite (LT-38) exercises every §4 condition against both backends and asserts `errors.Is` against the correct sentinel, per `decisions/error-semantics.md`'s "mandatory per-backend translation, verified by an explicit test case per backend."
20	- [ ] No raw driver error text (including a Postgres `DETAIL`/`Key (...)=(...)` line or a SQLite extended-code message) is ever wrapped, logged, or returned past the translation boundary — per `decisions/error-semantics.md` F50. Enforcement site: the translation function sanitizes to structured code + our constraint name before anything is wrapped for the log; a test asserting a constraint-violation error's logged/wrapped form contains no literal column value.
21	- [ ] `Upsert`'s `ON CONFLICT`/`ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (or SQLite equivalent) explicitly excludes `created_at` from the update-set list — per 05's Oren-finding-#3 ruling. Enforcement site: the SQL/query statement itself, plus a unit test asserting `created_at` survives a re-hydration `Upsert` unchanged, one test per backend (not one test assumed to cover both).
22	- [ ] `CreateProfileWithCredential` writes both rows in one transaction with `c.UserID` ignored on input and set from the newly created profile; on any failure, neither row exists. Enforcement site: a unit test per backend that fails the credential write deliberately (e.g. a duplicate username) and asserts the profile row does not exist afterward.
23	
24	## LT-36-specific acceptance criteria (Postgres + CockroachDB)
25	
26	- [ ] Every write method (`Create`, `Update`, `Upsert`, `Delete`, `CreateProfileWithCredential`) runs through the `withRetry` wrapper described in `decisions/error-semantics.md` — an unexported helper inside the `postgres` package, not in the composite and not above the interface boundary. Enforcement site: the wrapper is the single call path every write method uses; grep confirms no write method executes a statement outside it.
27	- [ ] The wrapper retries only when the implementation's unexported `engine` field (set by `New` from the driver string) is `"cockroachdb"`; on `"postgres"` it calls the function once. Enforcement site: a unit test constructing both engine values and asserting call count under a forced-retryable and a non-retryable condition.
28	- [ ] The retry wrapper actually retries a genuine SQLSTATE `40001` (serialization failure) against real CockroachDB, not a mocked/simulated one. Enforcement site: the conformance suite (LT-38) exercises a real retryable condition against a CockroachDB testcontainer/instance, per `backlog.md`'s own acceptance criterion for this story.
29	- [ ] `Search` opens no explicit transaction and is not wrapped by `withRetry` — it's read-only and per `decisions/error-semantics.md`'s ruling never encounters `40001`. Enforcement site: code review confirming `Search`'s implementation has no `withRetry` call.
30	- [ ] Fuzzy name search uses `LOWER(name) LIKE ?` matching the GIN expression index, with explicit `%`/`_`/`\` escaping on caller input. Enforcement site: query construction plus a unit test with a search term containing a literal `%` or `_`, asserting it's treated as a literal character, not a wildcard.
31	
32	## LT-37-specific acceptance criteria (SQLite)
33	
34	- [ ] `Create` generates the UUID in Go before insert (SQLite has no server-side generation) — enforcement site: the `Create` implementation, verified by the conformance suite asserting a populated `ID` comes back structurally identical to the Postgres path (same format, non-empty, DAO-generated either way).
35	- [ ] Search folds case **explicitly** — `LOWER(col) LIKE LOWER(pattern)` — rather than relying on SQLite's default ASCII case-insensitive `LIKE`, so the behavior is asserted by this package's own code, not an accident of the engine default. Enforcement site: the query construction plus the conformance suite's cross-backend result-set comparison (same rows, same order, for ASCII input, per `multi-db-strategy.md`'s "What Search does on SQLite" section).
36	- [ ] The documented non-ASCII case-folding divergence (Postgres `lower()` is locale-aware, SQLite's is ASCII-only) is **not silently accepted as an untested gap** — it must appear as an explicit, named exception in this package's own documentation (a doc comment on the search implementation), not only in `multi-db-strategy.md`'s prose, so a reader of the code alone sees the limitation. Enforcement site: doc comment in the SQLite search implementation, cross-referenced to `multi-db-strategy.md`.
37	- [ ] `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE`/`FOREIGNKEY`/`CHECK`/`PRIMARYKEY` map to the same sentinels as the Postgres package's SQLSTATE codes, disambiguated by the same DDL constraint names (not by table/column position, which can drift independently between the two schemas). Enforcement site: the translation table, verified by the conformance suite's sentinel-parity assertion across both backends for the same logical condition.
38	
39	## Non-goals
40	
41	- LT-38 (the conformance suite itself) is not this pair's story to write — but its assertions are cited above as the actual enforcement site for several criteria in both. If LT-38 hasn't landed by the time LT-36/LT-37 are otherwise ready, those criteria are pending, not waived — flagged explicitly in the PO review script below, not silently marked done.
42	- No handler/API-layer work — `internal/api` calling into these packages is a later story.
43	- No production connection-pool tuning, migration-execution wiring, or observability — infra/DevOps scope.
44	
45	## PO review script
46	
47	1. Confirm both PRs build and their own unit tests pass in CI (once 04's pipeline exists — flag as pending otherwise, per LT-34's precedent).
48	2. Confirm the conformance suite (LT-38, if it exists yet) runs against both backends in CI and is green; if LT-38 hasn't been pulled yet, explicitly mark every criterion above whose enforcement site is "the conformance suite" as **pending**, not accepted — do not accept LT-36/LT-37 on code review alone for those criteria.
49	3. Grep both packages for `strings.Contains` applied to an error value — must return nothing.
50	4. Spot-check the `Upsert` SQL/query in both packages for the `created_at`-exclusion; confirm a unit test exists per backend.
51	5. For LT-36: confirm the `withRetry` wrapper's engine-conditional behavior in code, and confirm `Search`'s implementation has no retry-wrapper call.
52	6. For LT-37: read the SQLite search implementation's doc comment for the non-ASCII case-folding exception; confirm it's stated, not silent.
53	7. Confirm neither package's tests or logs contain a literal DB error string with embedded values (spot-check by triggering one constraint violation locally and reading what gets logged).
54

1	# Decision: Error semantics (wrap vs. sentinel)
2	
3	Pattern: rotating devil's advocate, decision 1 of 3 (seat: Oren Castellan). Proposer: Renata Cole.
4	
5	## Proposal
6	
7	Go 1.13+ error wrapping (`fmt.Errorf("...: %w", err)`) for propagating unexpected/lower-level errors up the call stack, plus a small set of exported sentinel errors (`dao.ErrNotFound`, `dao.ErrDuplicate`) for conditions callers are expected to branch on via `errors.Is`.
8	
9	## Objections — Oren Castellan (Detail Hawk)
10	
11	1. **Sentinel set must be closed and enumerated now**, not left open-ended — otherwise hires add ad hoc `ErrX` values across S2–S4 with no stable contract. Wants a fixed list plus a named owner and a propose-before-adding rule.
12	2. **Wrapped errors must never cross the HTTP boundary raw** — `err.Error()` on a wrapped DB error can leak driver text (DSN fragments, column/constraint names) to an API client. Wants an explicit translation function at the boundary; anything unmapped defaults to a generic 500 with no wrapped detail.
13	3. **`errors.Is` against a sentinel breaks silently if a backend returns a driver-native error instead** (e.g. `*pgconn.PgError` instead of `dao.ErrDuplicate`). Wants the `Repository` interface contract to state translation is *mandatory* per backend, verified by an S5 test case per backend.
14	4. **No stated rule for `context.DeadlineExceeded`/`context.Canceled`.** These aren't ordinary internal errors and shouldn't page as generic 500s. Wants them checked and mapped to a distinct response code before generic wrapping applies.
15	
16	## Ruling (Renata Cole)
17	
18	All four accepted; no decline.
19	
20	1. Sentinel set is closed and fixed now: `dao.ErrNotFound`, `dao.ErrDuplicate`, `dao.ErrInvalidInput`. Defined once in `internal/dao`. Rule: no hire adds a sentinel unilaterally — propose it to me with the one-line reason, I add it. This applies for the rest of the design/implementation phase.
21	2. `internal/api` never serializes `err.Error()` on an unmapped error. A translation function (`internal/api` boundary layer) maps the three sentinels to their HTTP status/safe message; anything else defaults to a generic 500 with no wrapped detail surfaced to the client. Wrapped errors are for logs only.
22	3. The `Repository` interface contract states translation of known driver-native errors to the shared sentinels is **mandatory**, not optional, per backend implementation. S5's test strategy gets an explicit line item: one test per backend proving the translation happens (e.g., a unique-constraint violation surfaces as `dao.ErrDuplicate`, not a raw driver type).
23	4. `context.DeadlineExceeded` and `context.Canceled` are checked explicitly and mapped to a distinct response (504 for deadline exceeded; treat client-cancel as a non-alerting outcome, not logged as a 500) before generic wrapping/translation applies.
24	
25	## Status
26	
27	Ruled. Carries into S2 (DAO sentinel definitions + per-backend translation) and S3 (handler-layer translation function, context-error handling).
28	
29	## Superseded in part — 05's contract lands (2026-09-12)
30	
31	05's `multi-db-strategy.md` §4 is the authoritative sentinel set and translation mechanism for the DAO layer; it supersedes the specific names and the translation rule this decision guessed at before the contract existed. What stands vs. what's replaced:
32	
33	- **Replaced:** the invented sentinel set (`ErrNotFound`/`ErrDuplicate`/`ErrInvalidInput`) is superseded by 05's actual seven: `ErrNotFound`, `ErrAlreadyExists`, `ErrDuplicateUsername`, `ErrInvalidMethod`, `ErrInvalidQuery`, `ErrInvalidArgument`, `ErrInvalidCredential`. These are 05's to own and extend (own-and-propose-before-adding now routes through them, not me, for anything DAO-internal) — my closed-set/no-ad-hoc-additions principle still applies, just to their list.
34	- **Replaced:** "match SQLSTATE only" is superseded by 05's corrected rule — match structured error codes (SQLSTATE on Postgres/CockroachDB, extended result codes on SQLite) plus constraint names we define ourselves in DDL, never free-text messages. This is a more complete version of objection 2's concern (leaking driver text), not a contradiction of it.
35	- **Stands unchanged:** the HTTP-boundary rule (objection 2 — `internal/api` never serializes `err.Error()` on an unmapped error; wrapped errors are for logs only) — this is 03's layer, not 05's, and 05's contract doesn't touch it. The context-cancellation rule (objection 4) also stands unchanged, same reason.
36	- **Stands, now backed by a real mechanism instead of a guess:** objection 3's demand for mandatory per-backend translation — 05's contract §4 gives the actual mapping table (condition → PG/CRDB code → SQLite code → sentinel), and §7's conformance suite makes "sentinel parity for every error condition in §4" a stated, required test — stronger than what this decision asked for.
37	
38	## Consistency-pass fixes (2026-09-12)
39	
40	**F7 — authn/authz failure mapping was unstated.** This decision covered DAO-sentinel and context-error mapping but never said what an authn failure (missing/invalid token) or an authz failure (scope check, or an `authorize()` policy denial) returns. Fixed: a missing or invalid token → `401`. A scope failure and an `authorize()` policy denial both → `403`, with an **identical client-visible response body** — the two are distinguished only in the audit log (`handoff-03-auth.md`'s "policy decision: allow/deny, and if deny, whether it was a scope failure or an authorize() denial" field), never in what the client sees. This closes the gap the consistency pass found in `handoff-03-auth.md:27,50`'s "distinct signals for detection" requirement.
41	
42	**F50 — the boundary-sanitization rule needed to name driver `DETAIL` text explicitly.** The translation function (ruling item 2, above) already stated that unmapped errors default to a generic 500 with no wrapped detail — but a Postgres CHECK or unique-violation error's `DETAIL` line (which echoes the failing row's actual column values, including PII) is exactly the kind of "wrapped detail" that rule exists to stop, and it wasn't named specifically enough for a reader to be sure it was covered. Stated explicitly now: **the translation layer sanitizes every driver error down to its structured code plus our own constraint name before wrapping it for the log — the raw driver error (including any `DETAIL`/`Key (...)=(...)` text) is never wrapped, logged, or passed through in any form.** This translation layer (05's §4 mapping, implemented in `internal/dao`) is the enforcement site; `handoff-04-secrets.md`'s never-log list gets the matching entry from 02.
43	
44	---
45	Model: sonnet (Oren Castellan, objection seat; Renata Cole, lead, proposer + ruling). Turns consumed: 1 hire turn + lead synthesis.
46

Splitting them keeps each story reviewable on its own and matches how the work will actually land as commits.

78:## S5. Conformance suite (05's requirement, cross-backend) (LT-38)
83:- [ ] The suite is written once against the interface and executed against all three backends from one test file/table, not duplicated per package.
