87:- [ ] CI (04's pipeline) runs this suite against every backend including CockroachDB, not just Postgres+SQLite — flagged to 04 if their pipeline doesn't.
101:- [ ] Implements `handoff-03-auth.md` v3's scope vocabulary and pagination/rate-limit numbers without re-deriving 02's authz-scoping reasoning.
102:- [ ] `authorize()` runs as an explicit call inside each handler after request parsing, before the DAO call — enforcement site: handler code structure, per the go-layout-debate.md-adjacent ruling recorded in this track's reply to 02. `authorize()` checks the `caller_referral`/caller-role data against the token-issuer's own datastore, not against 05's schema — 05 was never assigned that table (`handoff-03-auth.md` v3, F8).
103:- [ ] `profile:search` responses carry masked PII fields by default (e.g. phone as `***-***-1234`, address reduced to `locality`/`region` only); unmasked detail requires a separate, separately-audited follow-up call under `profile:read:own` or `profile:read:any` against the specific record id — enforcement site: the search-response serialization path, distinct from the read-response one (`handoff-03-auth.md` v3, resolves F6).
104:- [ ] Cursor-based pagination only, default page size 20, hard ceiling 50 — enforcement site: the pagination parameter parsing, rejecting any offset-based parameter. **The handler's 20/50 is the authoritative clamp, enforced before the DAO is ever called** — the DAO's own `Limit` bound (1..100, default 50 per `multi-db-strategy.md`) is a second, independent backstop only reachable if the handler clamp is bypassed, not a second policy a caller can rely on (consistency-pass F10: no document previously said which of the two governs).
105:- [ ] A search request with no filter field at all is rejected by this handler before it reaches the DAO — the DAO itself treats an all-nil `ProfileQuery` as legal (`multi-db-strategy.md`), so this handler-layer rejection is the only enforcement site for "no bare unconstrained search" (consistency-pass F10, other half owned by 02 in `handoff-03-auth.md`).
106:- [ ] A `profile:search` query is rejected outright, not truncated, if it would exceed the result cap unconstrained (e.g. a bare wildcard) — enforcement site: this handler's query-validation step, per `handoff-03-auth.md` v3's scope table (consistency-pass F52).
107:- [ ] Unmapped DAO errors never surface `err.Error()` to the client; only the mapped sentinels' safe messages do — enforcement site: the boundary translation function from `decisions/error-semantics.md`. Authn failures (missing/invalid token) return 401; scope failures and `authorize()` policy denials both return 403 with an identical client body, distinguished only in the audit log (`decisions/error-semantics.md`, consistency-pass F7).
108:- [ ] `profile:read:any` requests carry a `reason_code` from the closed enum on every call, logged with the audit record.
109:- [ ] When `api-service` calls `idp-connector` on a caller's behalf, it obtains a `connector:identity-lookup`-scoped token first, using its own `CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID`/`CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET` credential (`PLANNING.md` config surface) — enforcement site: this handler-side outbound-client code, distinct from S8's idp-connector-side handlers, which only validate that token, not issue or hold this credential.
123:- [ ] Implements 02's token scheme without re-deriving the no-refresh/no-denylist reasoning in `api-auth-design.md`.
124:- [ ] Rejects any token whose `aud` doesn't match this service's audience — enforcement site: the claim-validation step, with a unit test using a mismatched-audience token.
125:- [ ] Verifies with the public key only; the private signing key never touches this binary's code path — enforcement site: dependency/import check (no signing-key material referenced in `internal/api`).
126:- [ ] **Pins the expected signing algorithm and rejects any token whose header claims a different one**, including `alg: none` and a symmetric-algorithm downgrade using the public key as an HMAC secret (RFC 8725 §3.1) — enforcement site: explicit algorithm check in the JWT verification call, not left to the library's default (`handoff-03-auth.md` v3 names this as a common library footgun).
127:- [ ] The token-issuing endpoint (the `APP_MODE=issuer` mode) has its own failed-grant rate limiting and progressive backoff per `client_id` and per source IP, separate from the resource-API limits below, with alerting on a sustained run of failed grants against one `client_id` — enforcement site: issuer-mode middleware, distinct from the verifier-mode rate limiter (`handoff-03-auth.md` v3).
128:- [ ] Per-client-credential rate limits (60 req/min read, 10 req/min search) and the cumulative distinct-record-touch counter (rolling 24h) are enforced and alert on sustained near-limit usage, not just throttle — enforcement site: the rate-limit middleware plus its own test.
129:- [ ] The deadline budget for a request (context propagation into the DAO layer) is sized to cover realistic p99 DAO latency, per `decisions/context-propagation.md`'s ruling — stated as a concrete number in this story's implementation, not left as "some deadline."

60:1. Implement `ProfileRepository`, `CredentialRepository`, `AuthMethodRepository` per the interface contract above, once there's a go-ahead to write code — don't redesign the contract, flag back to this track if it doesn't fit.
61:2. Two packages: `postgres` (serves Postgres + CockroachDB) and `sqlite`. No third implementation needed unless a fourth backend is added to the assignment.
62:3. Use the schema shape above as the starting migration content for both backend families.
63:4. Never let `UserCredential` and `UserProfile` fields mix in either implementation, even as an internal convenience struct — that boundary is a governance control (see `pii-governance.md`), not just a modeling choice.
339:1. **`ErrAlreadyExists` is real but not caller-reachable.** Because no caller supplies an ID, a primary-key collision can only arise from a UUID collision or a duplicated retry — never from caller error. It stays in the sentinel set and stays translated (it is one branch in the error mapping), but it is documented as an **internal-error signal, not a caller contract**, and the conformance suite is **not** required to exercise it. 03 should not spend effort constructing a test for a path a caller cannot reach.
341:2. **`Limit == 0` means "default 50", and requesting zero rows is out of scope.** The review correctly noted this is the same zero-value trap the pointer-means-absent convention avoids elsewhere in the struct. Accepted as a deliberate exception rather than fixed: a count-without-rows query is a *different operation*, and if it is ever wanted it arrives as an explicit `Count(ctx, ProfileQuery)` method — not as a magic value of `Limit`. `total` already gives a caller the count alongside the smallest useful page.
343:3. **`Upsert` must not overwrite `created_at`.** Its `ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE SET` list excludes `created_at` explicitly. Without that, every IDP re-hydration would silently reset the creation timestamp — on a column `pii-governance.md` keys retention to. This was an omission that would have produced a silent, retention-affecting bug, and it is now stated at both the method table and here.
490:1. **PII/credential separation.** `user_credential` carries no name, phone or address; `user_profile` carries no secret. This is a governance control, not normalization convenience — a breach of one table must not expose the other class of data (`pii-governance.md`). *Enforcement: the column sets in §5, the three-accessor split in §1, and a conformance test asserting neither struct gains a field from the other.*
491:2. **Cascade delete from profile to credential, and its asymmetry.** Deleting a profile destroys its credentials; the account ceases to exist. **The reverse does not hold** — deleting a credential leaves the profile and its PII standing on the `direct` retention clock. Deliberate. If "removing your last credential deletes the account" is wanted, that is a product policy decision, not a schema one, and it is currently *not* what the schema does. *Enforcement: `ON DELETE CASCADE` on the FK; the asymmetry is stated in `pii-governance.md`.*
492:3. **Case, collation and folding.** `ORDER BY LOWER(name) COLLATE "C" ASC, id ASC`, fixed and not caller-configurable, with collation named in DDL as well — `COLLATE "C"` on Postgres, `BINARY` on SQLite. Without this, page 1 of a search returns *different rows* per backend, because Postgres sorts by database collation while SQLite's default byte order puts every uppercase letter before every lowercase one. Cost accepted and named: `C` is byte order, so non-English names sort in an order a human would call wrong — identical-across-backends beats locale-pretty for a system whose whole premise is one contract over three engines. *Enforcement: the DDL collation clause, the `ORDER BY` expression, and a conformance test asserting identical ordering across backends.*
493:4. **`LIKE` escaping.** The DAO escapes `%`, `_` and `\` in caller input and emits an explicit `ESCAPE '\'` on both backends — Postgres has a default backslash escape, SQLite has none unless given. Without this, a search for `50%` matches every row. *Enforcement: the DAO's query construction, plus a conformance test using a term containing `%`.*
494:5. **`Country` normalization at write, not only at read.** Upper-casing the query without upper-casing the table makes a stored `us` silently invisible to a search for `US`. SQLite's `upper()` is ASCII-only — sufficient for alpha-2 codes, written down so nobody assumes it generalizes. *Enforcement: the `CHECK` constraint in §5 plus write-side normalization in both implementations.*
495:6. **UUID primary keys on every backend.** Required for CockroachDB (sequential keys create write hotspots on a single range), supported on Postgres, app-generated on SQLite. Settled by sourced research; do not reopen. *Enforcement: the column types in §5.*
496:7. **`auth_method` as a lookup table, not a native enum.** Adding a method is an `INSERT`, not a migration and not a deploy. *Enforcement: the FK from `user_credential.method_id`; see `migration-approach.md` §4.*
497:8. **The `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE` upsert form.** The one syntax all three backends accept; CockroachDB's `UPSERT` shorthand is deliberately unused to avoid a second dialect. *Enforcement: `Upsert`'s single query shape.*
498:9. **The two-package split** (`postgres` serving Postgres + CockroachDB, `sqlite` separate) and the engine-aware retry seam inside the former. *Enforcement: package boundaries; `decisions/multi-db-abstraction.md`.*
499:10. **`is_active` on `auth_method`.** `ErrInvalidMethod` is documented as "unknown **or inactive**", and the foreign key catches only *unknown* — a cold read applied item 11's own standard back to this document and found it failing its test. `is_active = false` has no DDL enforcement site and cannot have one, for the same reason as item 11: a `CHECK` on `user_credential` cannot read `auth_method`. *Enforcement: the DAO checks `is_active` before insert and returns `ErrInvalidMethod`, plus a conformance test that creates a credential against a deactivated method and asserts the sentinel.* Deactivating a method deliberately does **not** invalidate existing credentials — it prevents new ones.
500:11. **`deletion_log` carries no PII.** It exists to prove a deletion happened after the row is gone; a log containing the name and address of everyone whose data was deleted would be the retention policy's own failure mode wearing an audit trail's clothes. `profile_id` deliberately has no FK, because the row it names is meant not to exist. *Enforcement: the column set in §5, plus a conformance test asserting no PII-shaped column exists on the table.*
501:12. **The `requires_secret` ↔ `secret_state` relationship.** A credential whose method has `requires_secret = false` must not carry `secret_state = 'set'`. **This one cannot be a DDL constraint** — `CHECK` cannot reference another table — so its enforcement site is DAO-layer validation returning `ErrInvalidCredential`, plus a conformance test. Named honestly as one of the two weakest sites in this contract (the other is item 10, `is_active`), because the discipline is worthless if it only records the cases where a clean site existed.

1	# LT-38 refinement — S5: conformance suite (cross-backend behavioral conformance)
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03). **Source:** `03-engineering-delivery/backlog.md` S5 (LT-38).
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why pulled now
8	
9	LT-36 and LT-37's refinement (see `refinement/LT-36-LT-37.md`) cited this suite as the sole enforcement site for roughly half their own acceptance criteria and added a hard rule that neither story can be marked Done until LT-38 lands and those criteria actually pass. Pulling LT-38 now is what closes that gap — it's the next dependency in the chain (`Depends on: S3, S4`), and refining it now means it's ready to run the moment either backend PR lands, rather than sitting as a bottleneck after the fact.
10	
11	## Correction found before drafting (citation error in `backlog.md` itself, not introduced here)
12	
13	`backlog.md`'s S5 description cites "the secret-state rules (§5/§6.11)" for the `requires_secret`/`secret_state` relationship. Checked against `multi-db-strategy.md` directly: **§6.11 is item 11 in §6's numbered list — "`deletion_log` carries no PII" — a different guarantee entirely.** The `requires_secret`/`secret_state` relationship is item 12 (unlabeled with an explicit "§6.12" in the source doc's own prose, but numerically next in the same list, referred to there only as "item 12"). `is_active`'s citation as §6.10 is correct — that one checks out exactly. Fixed below by citing item 12 correctly and not repeating the backlog's §6.11 mislabel.
14	
15	## Acceptance outcome
16	
17	No public-URL surface (same as LT-35/36/37) — this is a test suite, not a deployable behavior change. "Done" means the suite exists once, runs against all three backends from one table-driven test, and every guarantee below is asserted as a specific, named test case (not a general "seems to work" pass) — modeled on the frozen-checksum-plus-structural-diff pattern Oren built for LT-35's conformance test, since 05's own §7 explicitly warns that signature conformance (LT-35) proves nothing about behavioral conformance (this story) — two implementations can match every declaration and still return different rows for the same call.
18	
19	## Acceptance criteria (restated from `backlog.md` S5, corrected and expanded)
20	
21	- [ ] The suite is written once against the `Repository` interface and executed against all three backends (postgres, cockroachdb, sqlite) from one table-driven test file, not duplicated per package. Enforcement site: a single test function parameterized over backend, not three near-identical copies.
22	- [ ] Asserts identical `Search` result sets and ordering across backends for ASCII input — this suite is `multi-db-strategy.md`'s named enforcement site for this guarantee; there is no other. Named test case: a fixed dataset seeded identically on all three backends, a fixed query, asserting row-for-row identical results and order.
23	- [ ] Asserts `errors.Is` sentinel parity for every condition in the §4 translation table, across all three backends — named test case per condition (not one combined test), consistent with LT-36/LT-37's "not-Done-until-LT-38" dependency: this is the specific enforcement site those stories were waiting on.
24	- [ ] Asserts the `requires_secret`/`secret_state` relationship (§6, item 12 — corrected citation, see above) as a named test: creating a credential with `secret_state = 'set'` against a method whose `requires_secret = false` returns `ErrInvalidCredential`, on all three backends.
25	- [ ] Asserts `is_active` enforcement (§6.10) as a named test: creating a credential against a deactivated `auth_method` returns `ErrInvalidMethod`, on all three backends — per the doc's own note that deactivation must not invalidate *existing* credentials, so the test also asserts an existing credential against a since-deactivated method still reads back successfully.
26	- [ ] Asserts empty-string rejection on every `*string` filter field in `ProfileQuery` — one named test case per field (`Name`, `Phone`, `Region`, `Country`), not one combined test, per the "absent, never empty" convention.
27	- [ ] Retention/deletion assertions from `multi-db-strategy.md` §7's own minimum list, each as a named test case: a zero-value `RetentionClass` (`""`) returns `ErrInvalidArgument`; `maxRows` outside `1..10_000` returns `ErrInvalidArgument`; `OldestSurvivingAt` is non-nil when rows remain (including when nothing was old enough to delete) and nil only when the class is empty; `Drained` is false while deletable rows remain at the batch limit and true on the final batch; every delete writes exactly one `deletion_log` row in the same transaction with the reason code the method itself asserts; an already-cancelled context returns before deleting anything; `deletion_log` carries no PII-shaped column (§6.11 — correctly cited here, this is the actual §6.11 guarantee).
28	- [ ] The CockroachDB-specific retry path is exercised for real: at least one test forces a genuine SQLSTATE `40001` (e.g. two concurrent transactions racing on the same row) against a real CockroachDB instance/testcontainer and asserts the operation succeeds after retry, not a mocked retry.
29	- [ ] CI (04's pipeline) runs this suite against every backend including CockroachDB, not just Postgres+SQLite — flagged to 04 if their pipeline configuration doesn't do this; enforcement site is the CI job matrix/config itself, not this suite's own code.
30	- [ ] Following the model Oren built for LT-35: the suite's test data / expected-behavior assertions state, in a comment, which parts are derived directly from `multi-db-strategy.md`'s prose (the guarantees list in §7 and the numbered items in §6) and, where practical, carry the same frozen-copy-plus-checksum protection so a later revision to those sections forces a human re-check rather than the suite silently asserting a stale guarantee. Scoped down from LT-35's version since this suite asserts *behavior*, not *signatures* — a full checksum-gated transcription may not be practical for prose guarantees the way it was for Go signatures; at minimum, each test case's doc comment names the specific `multi-db-strategy.md` line/item it enforces, so a future doc change is at least greppable.
31	
32	## Non-goals
33	
34	- No new backend implementation — this story only tests LT-36/LT-37's output.
35	- No handler/API-layer testing — that's LT-39 and later, against fakes per `decisions/test-double-strategy.md`, not this suite.
36	- No performance/load testing — "identical" here means identical correctness (rows, order, errors), not identical latency; SQLite's full-table-scan search is explicitly *not* required to be as fast as Postgres's indexed search, only as correct.

1	# Deploy Path — Discovery Findings
2	
3	Written per team-lead's agile-loop brief. Answers: how do we reach the cluster, how does GitHub Actions get code onto it, what does the public URL look like.
4	
5	## 1. What the cluster actually is — correction to the planning-phase narrative
6	
7	The planning-phase docs (`../PLAN.md`, `handoff-04-secrets.md`) described the deployment target as a "lab/dev cluster" reachable only through an external operator agent ("amber-kubernetes"). Direct discovery on this machine found something more specific and more consequential:
8	
9	- This machine already holds a working `kubectl` context (`dev`, cluster `uplift-kub`, 5 nodes, `kubeconfig` at `~/.kube/config`) with direct access — no SSH hop, no operator agent needed to *reach* it.
10	- The "OpenClaw CI/CD in Kubernetes dev cluster" session named in the brief is not currently listed by `ListAgents` — not contacted, since direct access already exists and answers the question it would have answered.
11	- **This is a real, live, multi-tenant cluster, not an isolated sandbox.** Namespaces include `bitwarden` (Warren's actual password manager, `bitwarden.uplifttech.org`), `homeassistant`, `kafka`, `cert-manager`, `backup`, and several real business sites (`salemforensics.com`, `mc.uplifttech.org`/`acrimoniousmc.com`, `calcom.uplifttech.org`, `floracore.uplifttech.org`). All public-facing services follow one pattern: an `Ingress` (class `nginx`) under a real hostname, TLS via `cert-manager`'s `letsencrypt-prod` `ClusterIssuer`, routed through one shared `ingress-nginx-controller` `Service` (`LoadBalancer`, internal IP `192.168.1.200`, presumably port-forwarded to the internet at the router — not verified from here, and not something to change).
12	- **Confirmed with Warren directly before proceeding** (this track paused and asked, given the mismatch between the planning-phase "isolated lab cluster" framing and what discovery found): deploy to this real cluster, in an isolated namespace, with a public tunnel/URL, is approved.
13	
14	**Consequence for this design:** every prior "lab cluster, nothing at stake" assumption is replaced with "shared production infrastructure serving real, unrelated services — isolate aggressively." Namespace, RBAC, and resource limits below are sized for that, not for a sandbox.
15	
16	## 2. GitHub Actions → cluster: self-hosted runner already exists
17	
18	GitHub-hosted runners can't reach a LAN cluster; the choice is a self-hosted runner or GitOps pull. **This cluster already runs Actions Runner Controller (ARC):**
19	- `arc-system` namespace: `arc-gha-rs-controller` (the ARC controller itself).
20	- `arc-runners` namespace: an `AutoscalingRunnerSet` named `k8s-linux-x64` (min 1, max 4 runners), with one runner Pod live.
21	
22	This runner set's current GitHub registration target (which org/repo it's scoped to) isn't determined from here without reading a Secret, and isn't assumed to already point at `wtvamp/loginid-takehome` — that's a question for whoever manages this ARC install, not something to touch. **Decision: do not repurpose the existing runner set.** Deploying this take-home's images and manifests needs a `kubectl apply` from inside the cluster's network with a scoped ServiceAccount — reusing an already-provisioned runner set that may serve other repos/workloads would mean this take-home's deploy job runs with whatever permissions that runner already has, which is exactly the shared-blast-radius problem this project's own designs (NetworkPolicy, RBAC-per-service) have been arguing against. Instead: **a second, narrowly-scoped `AutoscalingRunnerSet`**, registered to `wtvamp/loginid-takehome` specifically, in its own namespace, with a ServiceAccount whose RBAC is limited to the take-home's own namespace only (`get`/`list`/`create`/`update`/`patch` on `Deployments`/`Services`/`Ingresses`/`NetworkPolicies`/`Secrets`-by-name in that namespace, nothing cluster-scoped). This is additive to the cluster's existing ARC controller (one controller can run multiple scale sets), not a competing install.
23	
24	## 3. Namespace and isolation
25	
26	New namespace: `loginid-takehome`. Everything in `./containerization-design.md`'s manifests (both Deployments, the issuer Deployment, both Services, the `NetworkPolicy`) targets this namespace, not the placeholder `loginid-poc` used in the design-phase sketch — that name gets corrected in this pass. Resource limits from `./containerization-design.md` stay as designed (they were sized for "a two-service take-home," which is even more clearly correct now that the cluster is shared with real workloads, not less). A `ResourceQuota` on the namespace is a cheap additional backstop worth adding given the shared-cluster finding — caps total CPU/memory the whole take-home can ever consume regardless of what any single manifest requests.
27	
28	## 4. Public URL
29	
30	Following the cluster's existing, uniform pattern rather than inventing a new one (a Cloudflare Tunnel or similar was the planning-phase's guess; the cluster doesn't use one — every existing public service is a plain `Ingress` + `cert-manager` + the shared `ingress-nginx` `LoadBalancer`):
31	
32	- **Ingress host:** `loginid-takehome.uplifttech.org`, `Ingress` class `nginx`, TLS via `letsencrypt-prod`, matching every other app in the cluster.
33	- **Open item, not resolvable from this session:** the DNS record for that hostname doesn't currently resolve (checked; no answer). Either it needs adding at whatever DNS provider hosts `uplifttech.org` (Warren's call — this track has no DNS credentials and isn't requesting them) or it already resolves via a wildcard this check didn't hit correctly; needs one confirmation before the first deploy's public URL is real rather than theoretical. Flagging to Naomi/PO and Dana now rather than discovering it at the acceptance-script step.
34	- **Routes:** `/api/*` (or a path prefix TBD with 03) → `api-service`; the issuer's endpoints (`/auth/token`, `/.well-known/jwks.json` or similar, per 02/03's final route naming) → `api-service-issuer`. `idp-connector` gets no route — it's never publicly reachable, per its own design.
35	- **Acceptance-script shape this enables:** `https://loginid-takehome.uplifttech.org/healthz` (and an equivalent connector-side path if 03 exposes one) returning up + the deployed commit SHA, per team-lead's target script — `/healthz` already exists in the containerization design and 03's `internal/app` build-info work (per PM's note on LT-34) is what supplies the SHA.
36	
37	## 5. What this changes in `./containerization-design.md` (applied in this pass)
38	
39	- `namespace: loginid-poc` → `namespace: loginid-takehome` throughout.
40	- New `Ingress` resource (host above, TLS via `letsencrypt-prod`) added alongside the existing Services.
41	- A namespace-level `ResourceQuota` added as a backstop.
42	- Everything else (two Deployments, the issuer split, `NetworkPolicy`, resource limits per container, the migration `Job`) is unchanged — those were designed correctly for "shared infrastructure, isolate aggressively" even under the earlier, wrong assumption that this was a private sandbox.
43	
44	## 6. CI/CD sequencing (per team-lead's split)
45	
46	The PR-check workflow (lint, build, `-race` tests, `govulncheck`) needs none of the above and ships first, on GitHub-hosted runners — no cluster access required. The merge-to-main workflow (build+push images to GHCR, apply manifests via the new scoped runner set, print the public URL + commit SHA in the job summary) follows once the runner set and namespace exist. Both live at `.github/workflows/` at the repo root.
47	
48	---
49	*AI tooling note: discovery run directly by Theo (Sonnet, this session) via `kubectl`, `dig`, and `ListAgents` — no external operator agent contact was needed or made. Confirmed with Warren directly (AskUserQuestion) before treating the real-cluster finding as a green light, given the mismatch with the planning-phase framing.*

24	
25	- [ ] Both images build multi-stage (distroless final stage, non-root, read-only root filesystem) and contain nothing but the binary (+ CA certs for `idp-connector`). Enforcement site: the Dockerfile's final `COPY` list.
26	- [ ] Neither Pod can write its own root filesystem at runtime. Enforcement site: `securityContext.readOnlyRootFilesystem: true`.
27	- [ ] `idp-connector` is unreachable except from `api-service` — enforced by a **default-deny `NetworkPolicy`**, since the cluster's namespace default is allow, not deny; this is not optional hardening on a shared cluster. Enforcement site: the `NetworkPolicy` ingress rule scoped to `api-service`'s pod selector.
28	- [ ] The JWT signing private key is mounted only into `api-service-issuer`'s Deployment, never `api-service`'s own — enforcement site: `api-service`'s manifest carries no volume/mount referencing that `Secret`, verified by manifest inspection, not RBAC alone.
29	- [ ] All manifests target `namespace: loginid-takehome` (corrected from the design-phase placeholder `loginid-poc`) — enforcement site: every manifest's `metadata.namespace`, grepped for the old placeholder returning nothing.
30	- [ ] A namespace-level `ResourceQuota` caps total CPU/memory the whole take-home can consume, independent of any single manifest's own `resources.limits` — added specifically because this is shared production infrastructure, per `decisions/deploy-path.md` §3. Enforcement site: the `ResourceQuota` object itself, applied to the namespace.
31	- [ ] A public `Ingress` (class `nginx`, TLS via `letsencrypt-prod`) routes `/healthz` (and whatever path prefix 03 finalizes for the rest of the API) to `api-service`, and issuer-specific routes to `api-service-issuer`. `idp-connector` gets **no route** — never publicly reachable, per its own design. Enforcement site: the `Ingress` resource's path rules.
32	- [ ] The migration `Job` runs with `parallelism: 1` and is gated by the CI pipeline's concurrency mechanism (LT-47), not Kubernetes ordering alone.
33	- [ ] The GitHub Actions deploy step uses a **second, narrowly-scoped `AutoscalingRunnerSet`** registered specifically to `wtvamp/loginid-takehome`, with RBAC limited to the `loginid-takehome` namespace only (no cluster-scoped permissions) — **not** the cluster's existing shared runner set, per `decisions/deploy-path.md` §2's blast-radius reasoning. Enforcement site: the runner set's own RBAC manifest, scoped by namespace, reviewed independently of the take-home's application manifests.

36	
37	- [ ] A lint or test failure blocks merge — required status check, not advisory.
38	- [ ] A CockroachDB-specific SQL incompatibility surfaces at PR time via a dedicated CockroachDB testcontainer job.
39	- [ ] Only one migration run can reach the migrate stage at a time, across concurrent deploys — enforced by the pipeline's own concurrency group/mutex on the deploy environment.
40	- [ ] No secret enters a build log or image layer — `gitleaks` stage plus the Dockerfile's minimal `COPY` list (LT-45).

43	- [ ] The job summary on a successful merge-to-main run states the public URL and the deployed commit SHA explicitly, in plain text — this is what makes LT-34's PO review script (and every later story's) executable without a human hunting through logs.

1	# LT-32 refinement — split the token issuer into its own Deployment with isolated signing-key custody
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03, `APP_MODE` binary support), Theo Bergman (04, manifest/Deployment split); design owner Marcus Ilori (02, `handoff-04-secrets.md`/F13). **Source:** `02-ai-security-architecture/backlog.md` S12 (LT-32).
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterparts Renata Cole and Theo Bergman.
6	
7	## Why this one, not LT-39, and why now
8	
9	Checked the actual dependency chain rather than assuming: `backlog.md` S7 (auth middleware, LT-40) lists "04: `APP_MODE` Deployment split" as a hard dependency — that's this story. S6 (the search/retrieve handlers, LT-39) doesn't list LT-32 or S7 as a formal dependency (it can be built and merged against fakes without either), but a handler that calls `authorize()` on claims nothing has yet populated isn't a **reviewable, working endpoint** — it's code that compiles and can't be correctly exercised end-to-end at the public URL. LT-32 is the actual bottleneck: nothing downstream (S7, and a genuinely working authenticated S6) can be truly deployed and reviewed without it. Confirmed by checking the live manifest directly (`deploy/manifests.yaml` on `main` as of this refinement): no `api-service-issuer` Deployment exists, no JWT signing-key mount — this story hasn't landed in any form yet, it isn't partially done.
10	
11	Per Jira's own resolved ambiguity (`LOG.md`: "LT-34 blocks LT-32"), LT-32 was gated on LT-34 landing — LT-34 is now implemented and deployed (review pending only on the build-SHA fix, unrelated to this story), so LT-32 is unblocked and ready to pull.
12	
13	## What this story actually is, restated plainly
14	
15	Not a new binary, not new business logic — a **Deployment-topology fix** closing a real security gap the consistency pass found (F13): `handoff-04-secrets.md` said the signing key "never touches `api-service`" while the manifest mounted it into every replica of one `api-service` Deployment. RBAC on a `Secret`'s `get` verb does nothing once the volume is already mounted into a running container — so the only real fix is a second Deployment of the same image, selected by `APP_MODE=issuer` vs. the default, with its own ServiceAccount that alone can mount the key.
16	
17	## Acceptance outcome (no new public-URL behavior yet, but it's the load-bearing gate before any is trustworthy)
18	
19	This story doesn't add a caller-visible endpoint — `/healthz` still looks the same. "Done" is verified by manifest inspection and a runtime check that the two Deployments' pods genuinely differ in what they can mount, not by curling something new.
20	
21	## Acceptance criteria (restated from `backlog.md` S12)
22	
23	- [ ] `api-service` (the Go binary) supports an `APP_MODE` environment variable selecting issuer vs. default (verifying) behavior from one binary/image — no second `cmd/` entrypoint, per `handoff-04-secrets.md`'s explicit "not a third binary" ruling. Enforcement site: `internal/config` reads `APP_MODE`, `internal/app` branches on it at startup.
24	- [ ] Two Kubernetes Deployments exist in the manifest (`api-service` and `api-service-issuer`), each with its own ServiceAccount. Enforcement site: `deploy/manifests.yaml`, two distinct `Deployment` objects.
25	- [ ] Only `api-service-issuer`'s pod spec mounts the JWT signing-key `Secret` — **physically absent, not just RBAC-denied, from `api-service`'s pod spec.** Enforcement site: manifest inspection of `api-service`'s volumes/volumeMounts list, which must contain no reference to that `Secret` at all — a reviewer checking RBAC alone would miss a mistakenly-shared mount, per F13's own finding that RBAC doesn't prevent this class of leak.
26	- [ ] RBAC (`Role`/`RoleBinding`) additionally restricts `get`/`list` on the signing-key `Secret` to `api-service-issuer`'s ServiceAccount specifically — a second, independent layer on top of the physical-absence criterion above, not a substitute for it.
27	- [ ] The `api-service` (verifying) Deployment holds only the public key material needed to verify, never the private key, regardless of `APP_MODE`'s runtime value — enforcement site: a startup-time check/test asserting the verifying binary's process has no path to the private key even if `APP_MODE` were misconfigured to `issuer` on that Deployment (defense against a manifest typo, not just a design intent).
28	- [ ] `containerization-design.md`'s single-Deployment mount (the line the consistency pass named) is removed from the design doc and replaced with the two-Deployment description, so a future reader of the design doc sees the corrected shape, not the superseded one.
29	- [ ] The issuer Deployment's public route (whatever path 02/03 finalize for token issuance — e.g. `/auth/token`) is added to the `Ingress`, routed to `api-service-issuer`, distinct from `api-service`'s existing routes; `idp-connector` remains unrouted, unaffected by this change.
30	
31	## Non-goals
32	
33	- No change to the JWT scheme itself (claims, TTL, `kid` rotation mechanics) — that's `api-auth-design.md`'s design and S7's (LT-40) implementation, not this story's.
34	- No auth middleware implementation (JWT verification, rate limiting) — that's S7 (LT-40), which depends on this story but is a separate pull.
35	- No handler-layer `authorize()` logic — that's S6 (LT-39), also downstream of this and S7.
36	- No change to `idp-connector` — it has no issuer/verifier split; this story only touches `api-service`.
37	
38	## PO review script
39	
40	1. Confirm `deploy/manifests.yaml` (or wherever the PR puts it) has two distinct `Deployment` objects for the `api-service` image, each with its own `ServiceAccount`.
41	2. Read `api-service-issuer`'s pod spec: confirm the signing-key `Secret` volume mount is present.
42	3. Read `api-service`'s (verifying) pod spec: confirm **no** reference to that `Secret` anywhere — no volume, no volumeMount, not even an unused one.
43	4. Read the `Role`/`RoleBinding` scoping `get`/`list` on the `Secret` to the issuer's ServiceAccount specifically, not both.
44	5. Confirm `containerization-design.md` no longer describes the single-Deployment mount the consistency pass flagged.
45	6. Once deployed: hit the issuer's route (once 02/03 finalize its path) and confirm it's reachable and distinct from `api-service`'s existing routes; confirm `idp-connector` is unaffected (still unrouted).
46	7. If feasible, a deliberate negative check: attempt (from outside, with no special access) to read the signing-key `Secret` via the verifying Deployment's own ServiceAccount token — should fail, confirming criteria 3+4 together, not just one or the other.
47

258:## S12 — Split the token issuer into its own Deployment with isolated signing-key custody (LT-32)
259-
260-**Status: open** (from `../decisions/cross-track-consistency.md` F13).
261-
262-**Description:** The pass caught a real contradiction: `handoff-04-secrets.md` said the JWT signing private key "never touches `api-service`" while also defining the issuer as "a mode of `api-service`," and 04's manifest resolved that by mounting the signing key into every replica of a single `api-service` Deployment — RBAC on a Secret's `get` verb does nothing to stop a volume already mounted into a running container. Fixed at the design level in `handoff-04-secrets.md` Assumption 2 and row 2: the issuer runs as a second Deployment of the same `api-service` image, selected by an environment variable (`APP_MODE=issuer` vs. default), with its own ServiceAccount that alone mounts the signing-key Secret. This story is the implementation and manifest work that design change requires — it did not exist as a story before the pass.
263-
264-**Acceptance criteria:**
265-- [ ] `api-service` supports an `APP_MODE` environment variable selecting issuer vs. verifying behavior from one binary/image.
266-- [ ] Two Kubernetes Deployments exist, each with its own ServiceAccount; only the issuer Deployment's pod spec mounts the JWT signing-key Secret — enforcement site: the manifest's volume mounts plus RBAC Role scoping `get`/`list` to the issuer's ServiceAccount.
267-- [ ] The verifying Deployment's pod spec contains no reference to the signing-key Secret at all (not just RBAC-denied — physically absent from its mounts).
268-- [ ] `containerization-design.md`'s mount at the lines the pass named is removed and replaced with the two-Deployment shape.
269-
270-**Depends on:** `handoff-04-secrets.md` v2 (this track); 03's `APP_MODE` config surface addition; 04's manifest change.
271-
272-**Model/effort:** sonnet, high (03 implementation); sonnet, high (04 manifest).

1-# Hand-off 02 → 04: Secrets inventory and never-log list
2-
3:Status: **v2** — v1's S7 objection turn and 04's Newcomer cold read are both resolved and folded in below; this revision additionally resolves four findings from the cross-track consistency pass (F12 this version label, F13 the signing-key custody contradiction, F15 a missing credential row, F50 two never-log gaps). Receiver: Theo Bergman, `../04-infra-devops/`. Written to the project's hand-off standard: 04 should be able to act on this without re-deriving the reasoning in `threat-model.md`, `api-auth-design.md`, or `connector-security.md`.
4-
5-## Assumptions this hand-off makes
6-
7-1. Deployment target is 04's **real Kubernetes lab cluster, reachable behind a public tunnel** (Warren's implementation go-ahead, 2026-09-12 — updated from a design-only reference). Delivery mechanism is therefore Kubernetes-native `Secret` objects unless 04 chooses an external store; every requirement below is written so it still holds if the mechanism changes. **The repository is now public** (`https://github.com/wtvamp/loginid-takehome`) — every requirement in this document that says "never in source or committed config" is now categorical, with no private-repo safety net.
8:2. Service topology is 03's published boundary (`../PLANNING.md`, Service boundaries): two binaries, `cmd/api-service` (Q1 DAO + Q2 REST API) and `cmd/idp-connector` (Q3), env-var configuration only. Variable names below are 03's; two are marked as pending this track's final scheme. **The token-issuing authorization-server function is a mode of `api-service`, not a third binary** — this take-home's scope doesn't justify a standalone authz server, and 03 has published no third `cmd/` entrypoint. **Corrected by the cross-track consistency pass (F13):** "a mode of the same binary" is not "the same Kubernetes Deployment," and the earlier text here was wrong to imply RBAC alone separates them — a Kubernetes Role restricting `get` on a Secret has no effect on a volume already mounted into a container, so if `api-service`'s one Deployment mounted the signing key, every replica (verification path included) would have it on disk regardless of RBAC. **The fix: the issuer runs as a second Deployment of the same `api-service` image**, selected by an environment variable 03 adds to the config surface (e.g. `APP_MODE=issuer` vs. the default, consistent with the surface's env-var-only, no-flags rule), with **its own ServiceAccount that alone mounts the signing-key Secret** (row #2) — the `api-service` Deployment serving verification/search mounts nothing from row #2. This is a request into 03's Deployment/mode surface, not a unilateral change on my part; flag to me if the mode-selection mechanism needs to differ.
9-3. An **external operator agent** — a human or automated actor outside this project, holding cluster-admin or namespace-admin rights on 04's real lab cluster, who applies Kubernetes manifests to it — is a privileged actor outside this service: anyone who can apply a manifest can read any Secret it mounts. Consequence for 04: manifests reference Secrets by name and never carry values; values are set out-of-band. This is a named trust boundary in `threat-model.md` Assumption 7, not a silent assumption; nothing further about who that operator is needs to be re-derived from 04's own files to act on this hand-off. As of this update, 04 has not yet made contact with that operator or deployed anything — `threat-model.md` carries the dated status.
10-4. **Updated 2026-09-12:** with Warren's implementation go-ahead, 04's build stories will deploy to and contact the operator of the real cluster named in Assumption 1 — this is no longer a hypothetical the document describes in the abstract. As of this update, that contact hasn't happened yet; `threat-model.md` Assumption 7 carries the current, dated status and gets updated the moment it changes.

14-| # | Secret | Used by | Injection requirement (02 sets it) | Rotation expectation | Kubernetes-native note (04's mechanism) | Never-log |

16-| 1 | Database credentials, carried inside `DB_DSN` | `api-service` | Runtime-injected; never in source, image, or committed config. **Required, not preferred: file mount via a `DB_DSN_FILE` convention** (03 to add alongside `DB_DSN`), because a DSN's embedded password is exposed by `kubectl describe`, crash dumps, and child-process environment exactly as much as any other secret in this table — there is no citation that makes `DB_DSN` a lesser case, so this track is not treating it as one | On credential rotation; service restart or reload acceptable | `Secret` mounted read-only as a file, read via `DB_DSN_FILE`; env-var `DB_DSN` is a fallback only until 03 ships the file-based convention, not a permanent equal option | Yes — the DSN contains the password; log the driver name and host at most |
17:| 2 | JWT signing **private** key (RS256/ES256), per `api-auth-design.md` | The issuer Deployment (`api-service` image, `APP_MODE=issuer`) only — **not** the `api-service` Deployment that verifies with the public key; these are two Deployments of one image, not one Deployment doing both (F13 fix) | Runtime-injected as a **file**, never an env var; asymmetric so the verifying Deployment never holds it | Key rotation with a `kid` claim; publish old and new public keys together during overlap; TTL of tokens (5–15 min) bounds the overlap window | `Secret` mounted read-only as a file, **only into the issuer Deployment's pod spec** (not the verifying Deployment's); RBAC restricts `get`/`list` to the issuer's own ServiceAccount | Yes |
18-| 3 | JWT **public** key / JWKS | `api-service` middleware | Not a secret; may ship in config or be fetched from the issuer | Follows #2 | Plain `ConfigMap` or fetched URL; listed here so 04 does not over-protect it | No (but never log full tokens — see below) |
19-| 4 | API caller `client_secret`s (OAuth2 client-credentials) | Authorization server (verifies); callers hold their own copy | **Stored hashed** (Argon2id, same floor as `threat-model.md` Asset 1), never plaintext at rest; issued once at provisioning and not recoverable | Per-client rotation on demand; revoke by deleting the hash | Lives in the issuer's datastore, not a Kubernetes `Secret`; 04 delivers only that datastore's credentials (#1-shaped) | Yes |
20-| 5 | Connector's own vendor registration: `IDP_ABC_CLIENT_ID`, `IDP_ABC_CLIENT_SECRET` (and the XYZ pair if a second vendor is configured) | `idp-connector` | Runtime-injected; never in source or committed config; `client_id` is low-sensitivity but travels with the secret and gets the same handling | Per vendor's policy; expect manual rotation | One `Secret` per vendor so rotating ABC never touches XYZ; file mount preferred | Yes for the secret; keep the id out of logs too |
21-| 6 | Vendor `access_token` (returned by our own `/auth` to its authenticated caller, presented as a header on our own `/identity` — updated for F3's caller-holds-the-token model, seam re-run regression fix) | `api-service` holds it briefly between the two calls; `idp-connector` only ever sees it as a caller-presented header on a single `/identity` request, never fetches or holds it between calls itself | **Not a deployed secret** — transient on both sides, never cached, never persisted. Neither `api-service` nor `idp-connector` writes it to a datastore (S3's no-cache ruling, restated for the two-party model in `connector-security.md` §1) | Expires on its own; never persisted across restarts by either party | No `Secret` object anywhere in this flow — there is nothing here for 04 to provision | Yes — never, including error paths, on both sides of the exchange |
22-| 7 | Data-encryption key (KEK) for envelope encryption of any TOTP shared secrets | `api-service` (TOTP) only — **not a committed feature of this submission**; `user_credential.method` is modeled as an extensible lookup table (`../05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md`) that supports TOTP as a *possible* value, but no TOTP implementation is planned unless 03 builds one. **The vendor-token-cache use case for this row no longer exists**: S3's red/blue on the connector token lifecycle is closed (`decisions/connector-token-lifecycle-redblue.md`) — no cache by default, and the token is never held by the connector between calls at all under F3's model (row 6), so there is no vendor-token cache for a KEK to encrypt. This row is provisioned only if TOTP materializes — it is a contingency the inventory names in advance, not a live requirement for 04 to build now | Runtime-injected as a file; the KEK never leaves the process that decrypts | Rotation by re-wrapping DEKs; never requires re-hashing passwords (those are hashed, not encrypted) | `Secret` (file mount) or an external KMS if 04 prefers — the requirement is only that a KEK exists and is not in source | Yes |
23-| 7a | Credential to reach the external KMS, **only if 04 exercises the KMS alternative in row #7** (a role, API key, or client cert — whichever the chosen KMS uses) | Whichever binary holds the KEK reference (#7) | Same discipline as every other row here: runtime-injected, never in source, never provisioned ad hoc outside this table | Per the KMS provider's own rotation mechanism | Cloud-KMS access is typically workload-identity-based (no static credential to store) rather than a `Secret` object; if the chosen KMS instead requires a static credential, it gets its own `Secret` under the same RBAC discipline as row #2 | Yes |
24-| 8 | TLS serving private keys for `api-service` and `idp-connector` | Ingress / each binary, per 04's termination choice | Runtime-injected; TLS 1.2+ only; certificate validation never disabled in any environment (`connector-security.md` §2) | Per certificate lifetime; automated issuance preferred | `kubernetes.io/tls` `Secret`; issuance mechanism is 04's call | Yes |
25-| 9 | End-user's vendor password (`POST /auth` request body) | `idp-connector`, transient | Never persisted, never cached, held only for the vendor call | n/a | Nothing for 04 to deliver; listed so nobody creates a place for it | **Yes — under no circumstance, including stack traces** |
26-| 10 | `api-service`'s own client credential (`client_id`/`client_secret`) toward the issuer, scoped `connector:identity-lookup`/`aud: idp-connector-service` — required for `api-service` to call `idp-connector`'s own authenticated `/auth`/`/identity` (`connector-security.md` §5) | `api-service` (the Deployment serving question 2's API, acting as a caller of the connector on a user's behalf) | Runtime-injected; never in source or committed config — same discipline as row 5's vendor registration, added by the cross-track consistency pass (F15), which found this credential asserted as a requirement in `connector-security.md`/`handoff-03-auth.md` with no inventory row | Same per-client rotation as row 4 (revoke by deleting the issuer-side hash) | File mount, one `Secret`, `api-service`'s own ServiceAccount | Yes |

1	# LT-39 refinement — S6: REST handlers for search/retrieve user_profile (Q2)
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03). **Source:** `03-engineering-delivery/backlog.md` S6 (LT-39); `02-ai-security-architecture/handoff-03-auth.md` v3.
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why this one now
8	
9	LT-32 (issuer Deployment split) is refined and handed off, unblocking S7 (auth middleware, LT-40), which this story sits alongside. LT-39 itself formally depends only on S2 (merged) and 02's accepted hand-off — it doesn't block on LT-32/S7 in `backlog.md`'s own dependency graph, and its handlers can be built and merged against fakes right now, per `decisions/test-double-strategy.md`. Refining it now means it's ready the moment S7 lands, rather than sitting as the next bottleneck after that.
10	
11	## Acceptance outcome
12	
13	This is the first story that adds real, caller-visible REST behavior — not just a skeleton. But it depends on S7 (auth middleware) to be *truly* reviewable at the public URL with a real token; until S7 lands, "done" for this story specifically means: handlers implemented and tested against fakes, all criteria below satisfied in code and unit tests, with the actual live-URL, real-token exercise deferred to whenever S7 merges (same "verification pending" pattern as LT-36/LT-37 waiting on LT-38 — not waived, just sequenced).
14	
15	## Acceptance criteria (restated from `backlog.md` S6, cross-checked against `handoff-03-auth.md` v3)
16	
17	- [ ] Implements `handoff-03-auth.md` v3's scope vocabulary (`profile:read:own`, `profile:read:any`, `profile:search`) and pagination/rate-limit numbers without re-deriving 02's authz-scoping reasoning.
18	- [ ] `authorize(sub, scope, target)` runs as an explicit call inside each handler after request parsing, before the DAO call — not a second middleware, per v3's confirmed placement (it needs the parsed target id, which pre-routing middleware doesn't have). `authorize()` checks `caller_referral`/caller-role data against the token-issuer's own datastore, **not against 05's schema** — 05 was never assigned that table.
19	- [ ] `profile:search` responses carry masked PII fields by default (phone as `***-***-1234`, address reduced to `locality`/`region` only). Unmasked detail requires a separate, separately-audited follow-up call under `profile:read:own` or `profile:read:any` against the specific record id — **this reuses the existing scopes, it does not invent a fourth "expand" flag**; masking is a `profile:search`-response property, not a per-call parameter. Enforcement site: the search-response serialization path is a distinct code path from the read-response one, not the same serializer with a masking flag.
20	- [ ] **Pagination — clarified per v3's F-pag correction, since `backlog.md`'s original wording could be misread by an implementer:** the API-facing cursor is opaque to the caller — callers cannot construct or walk one by hand — but it may (and in the simplest correct implementation, does) encode the DAO's own `Offset` directly underneath. "Cursor-based pagination only" means **the caller-facing contract is opaque-cursor-only, not that the handler is forbidden from using the DAO's `Offset` field internally.** The actual anti-enumeration control is the cumulative distinct-record-touch counter (next bullet), not the cursor mechanism itself. Default page size 20, hard ceiling 50, enforced in the handler before the DAO is ever called — the DAO's own `Limit` bound (1..100, default 50) is a second, independent backstop only reachable if the handler clamp is bypassed, not a second policy a caller can rely on.
21	- [ ] A cumulative distinct-record-touch counter per caller per rolling 24h window is enforced, independent of per-minute rate limits, applying to **both** `profile:search` and `profile:read:any` unconditionally — a valid `reason_code` on `read:any` satisfies the per-call `authorize()` check but does **not** exempt the caller from this cumulative cap (v3's explicit fix to an earlier wording that made the counter a no-op for `read:any`). Enforcement site: a counting store incremented once per distinct record id returned, checked before the response is sent, alerting on threshold.
22	- [ ] A search request with no filter field at all is rejected by this handler with a 400 before it reaches the DAO — the DAO itself treats an all-nil `ProfileQuery` as legal, so this handler-layer rejection is the only enforcement site for "no bare unconstrained search."
23	- [ ] A `profile:search` query rejected for exceeding the result cap unconstrained (e.g. a bare wildcard) is rejected outright, not truncated — enforcement site: the same in-handler call site, checked before the query is built.
24	- [ ] Unmapped DAO errors never surface `err.Error()` to the client; only the mapped sentinels' safe messages do — per `decisions/error-semantics.md`'s boundary translation function. Authn failures (missing/invalid token) return 401; scope failures and `authorize()` policy denials both return 403 with an identical client-visible body, distinguished only in the audit log.
25	- [ ] `profile:read:any` requests carry a `reason_code` from the closed enum (`support_ticket`, `fraud_review`, `kyc_reverification`, `legal_hold`) on every call, logged with the audit record — adding a fifth value is a documented change to this enum, not a runtime config toggle.
26	- [ ] When `api-service` calls `idp-connector` on a caller's behalf, it obtains a `connector:identity-lookup`-scoped token first, using its own `CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID`/`CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET` credential — distinct from S8's idp-connector-side handlers, which only validate that token, not issue or hold this credential.
27	
28	## Non-goals
29	
30	- No auth middleware implementation — that's S7 (LT-40), a separate pull; this story's own tests use fakes for the auth context per `decisions/test-double-strategy.md`.
31	- No real backend I/O — built against fakes, per `decisions/test-double-strategy.md`; LT-36/LT-37/LT-38 land separately.
32	- No connector-side (`idp-connector`) handler work — that's S8, which only validates the token this story's handler obtains, not the same code.
33	- No rate-limit/counter *mechanism* choice (Redis vs. in-process) — that's Renata's implementation call, per v3's own "the mechanism is your call" framing.
34	
35	## PO review script
36	
37	1. Confirm `authorize()` is called explicitly inside each handler, after parsing, before the DAO call — not registered as middleware. Read the handler code directly.
38	2. Confirm the search-response serializer and the read-response serializer are genuinely distinct code paths (grep for two separate serialization functions, not one with a masking conditional).
39	3. Confirm the pagination cursor is opaque at the API boundary (no caller-suppliable offset/page-number parameter accepted) — but don't fail the story if the handler internally passes `Offset` to the DAO; that's the correct implementation per the F-pag clarification above.
40	4. Confirm the cumulative distinct-record-touch counter's test coverage includes a `profile:read:any` case with a valid `reason_code` still hitting the cap — this is the specific regression v3 fixed, so it needs its own test, not just a `profile:search` test.
41	5. Confirm a 401/403 test pair exists asserting identical response bodies for a scope failure vs. an `authorize()` denial, distinguished only in a separate audit-log assertion.
42	6. Once S7 lands and this is deployed: a live-URL check with a real client-credentials token, confirming an actual masked search response and an actual 401 on a missing/expired token — deferred, not waived, per the Acceptance outcome above.
43

## S6. REST handlers: search/retrieve user_profile (Q2) (LT-39)

**Description:** The client-facing handlers in `internal/api` implementing the assignment's Q2 (search and retrieve `user_profile`), built against S2's interfaces (fakes stand in for a real backend per `decisions/test-double-strategy.md`, so this doesn't wait on S3/S4). Implements the object-level `authorize(sub, scope, target)` call as an explicit in-handler step, not a second middleware, per the ruling in `handoff-03-auth.md` v3 (my own answer, confirmed by 02).

**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Implements `handoff-03-auth.md` v3's scope vocabulary and pagination/rate-limit numbers without re-deriving 02's authz-scoping reasoning.
- [ ] `authorize()` runs as an explicit call inside each handler after request parsing, before the DAO call — enforcement site: handler code structure, per the go-layout-debate.md-adjacent ruling recorded in this track's reply to 02. `authorize()` checks the `caller_referral`/caller-role data against the token-issuer's own datastore, not against 05's schema — 05 was never assigned that table (`handoff-03-auth.md` v3, F8).
- [ ] `profile:search` responses carry masked PII fields by default (e.g. phone as `***-***-1234`, address reduced to `locality`/`region` only); unmasked detail requires a separate, separately-audited follow-up call under `profile:read:own` or `profile:read:any` against the specific record id — enforcement site: the search-response serialization path, distinct from the read-response one (`handoff-03-auth.md` v3, resolves F6).
- [ ] Cursor-based pagination only, default page size 20, hard ceiling 50 — enforcement site: the pagination parameter parsing, rejecting any offset-based parameter. **The handler's 20/50 is the authoritative clamp, enforced before the DAO is ever called** — the DAO's own `Limit` bound (1..100, default 50 per `multi-db-strategy.md`) is a second, independent backstop only reachable if the handler clamp is bypassed, not a second policy a caller can rely on (consistency-pass F10: no document previously said which of the two governs).
- [ ] A search request with no filter field at all is rejected by this handler before it reaches the DAO — the DAO itself treats an all-nil `ProfileQuery` as legal (`multi-db-strategy.md`), so this handler-layer rejection is the only enforcement site for "no bare unconstrained search" (consistency-pass F10, other half owned by 02 in `handoff-03-auth.md`).
- [ ] A `profile:search` query is rejected outright, not truncated, if it would exceed the result cap unconstrained (e.g. a bare wildcard) — enforcement site: this handler's query-validation step, per `handoff-03-auth.md` v3's scope table (consistency-pass F52).
- [ ] Unmapped DAO errors never surface `err.Error()` to the client; only the mapped sentinels' safe messages do — enforcement site: the boundary translation function from `decisions/error-semantics.md`. Authn failures (missing/invalid token) return 401; scope failures and `authorize()` policy denials both return 403 with an identical client body, distinguished only in the audit log (`decisions/error-semantics.md`, consistency-pass F7).
- [ ] `profile:read:any` requests carry a `reason_code` from the closed enum on every call, logged with the audit record.
- [ ] When `api-service` calls `idp-connector` on a caller's behalf, it obtains a `connector:identity-lookup`-scoped token first, using its own `CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID`/`CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET` credential (`PLANNING.md` config surface) — enforcement site: this handler-side outbound-client code, distinct from S8's idp-connector-side handlers, which only validate that token, not issue or hold this credential.

**Depends on:** S2; **02: Auth scheme and authorization hand-off** (`handoff-03-auth.md` v3, accepted).
**Model/effort:** sonnet, high.
**Type:** Story.
**Labels:** `track-03`, `security-graded`, `no-code-yet`.

45:## Pagination, rate limits, decomposition resistance (requirements — numbers are fixed, mechanism is yours)
47:- **Opaque cursor at the API boundary** (resolves F-pag — the earlier "cursor-based pagination only, no offset-based paging" was wrong to imply offset itself is the enumeration risk: a keyset cursor permits the same page-by-page walk, just statefully; the actual control is the cumulative counter below, and 05's DAO contract is `Offset int`, not keyset). The API-facing cursor may encode the DAO's offset directly — callers cannot construct or walk one by hand, which is the property the boundary exists to give you, and the DAO's offset is never caller-visible. **Enforcement site: the cursor-encoding/decoding step in the handler layer**, opaque to the caller regardless of what it encodes underneath. Default page size 20, hard ceiling 50 (05's DAO ceiling is 10,000 — this API's ceiling is the tighter, caller-facing one; don't expose the DAO's).
49:- **Cumulative distinct-record-touch counter per caller per rolling 24h window**, tracked independently of per-minute rate — this is the control that actually resists decomposition-style enumeration (not the pagination scheme, per the F-pag correction above). **Applies to `profile:read:any` as well as `profile:search`, unconditionally on whether a `reason_code` is present** — a valid, logged `reason_code` satisfies the per-call `authorize()` policy check but does not exempt the caller from this cumulative cap (S8 review fix: the original wording made this counter a no-op for `read:any`, since that scope requires a `reason_code` on every call by design). **Enforcement site: a counting store (Redis, in-process with periodic flush, whatever fits your stack) incremented once per distinct record id returned, checked before the response is sent** — the mechanism is your call; the requirement is that it exists, applies to both scopes, and alerts on threshold, not just hard-blocks.

35:`reason_code` enum for `profile:read:any` (closed, versioned — adding a value is a documented change, not a runtime config toggle): `support_ticket`, `fraud_review`, `kyc_reverification`, `legal_hold`.
39:**Where `caller_referral` and the caller-role table live (resolves F8 — this data was previously asserted as "owned by `../05-data-ops/`" without 05 ever having received that assignment; 05's schema has no such table and shouldn't grow one for this).** Both live in the token-issuing authorization server's own datastore (the same one that holds the hashed `client_secret`s per `handoff-04-secrets.md` row #4), not in `05-data-ops`'s `user_profile`/`user_credential` schema. This is authorization-provisioning data — who a client credential is allowed to act on behalf of — not subject data, and keeping it out of 05's schema also keeps it out of that schema's PII-governance/retention rules, which don't apply to it. 05 owns nothing here; this hand-off's earlier wording was wrong to say otherwise.
41:**Response shape and masking (resolves F6 — 01's product requirement had no mechanism to bind to).** `profile:search` responses carry masked PII fields by default (e.g., a phone shown as `***-***-1234`, an address reduced to `locality`/`region` only) — unmasked, full-field detail is obtained only by a follow-up call under `profile:read:own` or `profile:read:any` against the specific record id returned by the search, which is its own separately-audited call per the audit-log section below. This uses scopes that already exist rather than inventing a fourth "expand" concept: masking is a `profile:search`-response property, not a per-call flag, and "tighter than search" is satisfied structurally because `read:own`/`read:any` are already narrower-provisioned scopes than `search`.
43:**Reject an empty search body before the DAO (resolves F10, first half).** A `profile:search` request with no `name` or `phone` filter at all is rejected by the handler with a 400 before it ever reaches the DAO — the DAO's own "an all-nil query is legal and returns every profile" behavior (`../05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md`) is a DAO-layer property this API must not expose directly. This is a shape-based rejection, distinct from and in addition to the count-based result-cap rejection above.

1	# LT-40 refinement — S7: auth middleware (JWT validation)
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03). **Source:** `03-engineering-delivery/backlog.md` S7 (LT-40); `02-ai-security-architecture/api-auth-design.md`, `handoff-03-auth.md` v3.
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why this one, now, and at priority
8	
9	This is the critical path per team-lead's direction: LT-39's handlers are code-review-passed but deliberately unwired (exposing them before real auth exists would serve unauthenticated PII), LT-32's issuer split is deployed and live at `/auth/token` (currently a 501 placeholder), and LT-34's live-URL story stays open until something real is reachable through auth. This story is what turns all of that into an actual, reviewable, authenticated public endpoint.
10	
11	## Correction to `backlog.md`'s own story text, found before drafting
12	
13	**S7's acceptance criterion 6 ("per-client-credential rate limits and the cumulative distinct-record-touch counter ... enforcement site: the rate-limit middleware plus its own test") is stale relative to work already merged.** `backlog.md` was written before LT-39 (PR #26) implemented `RateLimiter` and `TouchCounter` as explicit, tested checks called *inside* each handler (via a `Deps` struct), not as separate HTTP middleware — confirmed by reading `internal/api/auth.go`, `handlers.go`, `ratelimit.go`, `touchcounter.go` directly. Both are already built, both are already tested (including the two hard-deny regression tests from LT-39's own refinement), and both are already wired to whatever `AuthContext` the request context carries.
14	
15	**What this means for LT-40's actual scope:** this story does **not** rebuild rate limiting or the touch counter. Its job is narrower and already well-seamed: verify the JWT, populate `AuthContext` (via `WithAuthContext`, already exported by `internal/api`) with the token's `sub` and `scope` claims, and wire the resulting middleware into `cmd/api-service`'s router ahead of the already-built handlers. The 90-day-re-approval/lifecycle-revocation machinery (`api-auth-design.md`) and the issuer-side rate limiting (criterion 5, below) are the only genuinely new rate-limit-shaped work this story adds — the resource-API limits are already done.
16	
17	## Acceptance outcome
18	
19	"Done" for this story specifically is the first fully authenticated, live, reviewable REST endpoint: a real client-credentials token obtained from `/auth/token` (once its actual issuance logic lands — see Non-goals) or, in the interim, a token minted directly against the deployed signing key for review purposes, presented to `/profiles/search` or `/profiles/{id}` at the public URL, producing a masked search response or a 401/403/429 exactly as LT-39's tests predict.
20	
21	## Acceptance criteria (restated from `backlog.md` S7, corrected per the finding above)
22	
23	- [ ] Implements 02's token scheme without re-deriving the no-refresh/no-denylist reasoning in `api-auth-design.md` — no refresh tokens (client re-authenticates on expiry); TTL-only revocation (5–15 min), no denylist, an explicitly accepted trade-off, not to be re-litigated.
24	- [ ] Rejects any token whose `aud` doesn't match this service's audience — enforcement site: the claim-validation step, unit test with a mismatched-audience token.
25	- [ ] Verifies with the public key only; the private signing key never touches this binary's code path — enforcement site: dependency/import check, no signing-key material referenced anywhere in `internal/api` or the verifier's own binary path (this is already structurally true per LT-32's Deployment split; this criterion confirms the *code*, not just the *Deployment*, never imports signing-key material).
26	- [ ] **Pins the expected signing algorithm and rejects any token whose header claims a different one**, including `alg: none` and a symmetric-algorithm downgrade using the public key as an HMAC secret (RFC 8725 §3.1) — enforcement site: an explicit algorithm allowlist check in the JWT verification call, never left to the library's default. Unit tests: a token with `alg: none`, a token HMAC-signed using the public key bytes as the secret, both rejected.
27	- [ ] `kid`-based key rotation: the middleware selects the verification key by the token's `kid` header claim, supporting at least two concurrently valid public keys during a rotation overlap window — enforcement site: a unit test presenting tokens signed under two different `kid`s, both verifying successfully when both keys are configured.
28	- [ ] The middleware populates `internal/api.AuthContext` (via the already-exported `WithAuthContext`) with the token's `sub` and `scope` claims on success, and returns 401 (via LT-39's existing unauthorized-response path, not a new one) on any verification failure — **this is the actual integration seam with S6's work, not new response-shape design.**
29	- [ ] The token-issuing endpoint (`APP_MODE=issuer`, currently the 501 placeholder from LT-32) gets its own failed-grant rate limiting and progressive backoff per `client_id` and per source IP, separate from the resource-API limits (which are already built in `internal/api`) — with alerting on a sustained run of failed grants against one `client_id`. This is genuinely new work, not already covered by LT-39.
30	- [ ] The deadline budget for a request (context propagation into the DAO layer) is sized to cover realistic p99 DAO latency per `decisions/context-propagation.md`'s ruling — stated as a concrete number in this story's implementation, not left as "some deadline."
31	- [ ] The resource-API rate limiter and touch counter already built in LT-39 are wired to real, JWT-derived `AuthContext.Sub` values once this middleware populates them — **no new implementation, an integration check**: a live-URL test with a real token confirms the 60/10 req/min limits and the touch cap actually apply per real client identity, not per a test fake's hardcoded value.
32	
33	## Non-goals
34	
35	- **Real token issuance logic** (actually minting a signed JWT from valid client credentials at `/auth/token`) is explicitly out of scope for this story unless `backlog.md`/02 says otherwise — LT-32 only built the placeholder route and the signing-key custody; this story is the *verifier* side. Flagged as an open question below, since without it there's no way to obtain a real token for the live-URL review other than an out-of-band test token minted directly against the deployed key.
36	- No change to `internal/api`'s handler logic, masking, or error translation — that's LT-39, already merged.
37	- No change to the object-level `authorize()` policy datastore — still a stated seam (`Authorizer` interface), real implementation is a later story per `handoff-03-auth.md` v3's F8 resolution.
38	- No client-credential provisioning UI/API for new callers — out of scope for this take-home entirely, per `01-product-industry-research-design`'s track boundary.
39	
40	## Open question, flagged before hand-off rather than discovered at review time
41	
42	**Is there a real token-issuance implementation planned before or alongside this story, or does the live-URL review use an out-of-band test token?** `backlog.md` doesn't have a separate story for `/auth/token`'s actual grant logic (validate client_id/client_secret, mint a signed JWT) — LT-32 only built the route as a 501 placeholder. If issuance isn't a planned story, the PO review script below needs an out-of-band way to mint a real token against the deployed signing key for review purposes, which is a meaningfully different (and less representative) review than "call `/auth/token` for real." Flagging to Renata/team-lead now rather than discovering it when I try to run the review script.
43	
44	## PO review script
45	
46	1. Confirm the algorithm-allowlist and `kid`-rotation unit tests exist and pass — read the test names and assertions directly, not just "tests pass."
47	2. Confirm `AuthContext` population uses the already-exported `WithAuthContext`/`AuthContext` types from `internal/api`, not a parallel/duplicate type — grep for a second definition of anything auth-context-shaped.
48	3. Confirm the issuer-side rate limiting is a distinct code path from the resource-API rate limiter already in `internal/api/ratelimit.go` — this is genuinely new work, shouldn't reuse or be confused with the existing one.
49	4. Confirm the context-propagation deadline is a concrete, named number in code (e.g. a constant), not an inline magic value scattered across call sites.
50	5. Once deployed and a real (or, per the open question above, out-of-band) token is available: hit `/profiles/search` and `/profiles/{id}` at the live URL, confirming a masked response, a 401 on a missing/expired/wrong-audience token, a 403 on a wrong-scope token, and — if enough calls are made — an actual 429 once the touch cap is crossed. This is the live-URL review LT-34/LT-39 have both been waiting on.
